                Notice of Suspension of your provider registration (4-4GHG-1073)
              under section 73N of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013
                                      with immediate effect.


The National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS Act) gives me, as the Commissioner’s
delegate, the power to suspend the registration of a registered NDIS provider for a specified period,
no longer than thirty (30) days.

I have made the decision to suspend the registration of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, registration
number 4-4GHG-1073, for a period of 30 days, commencing from the date of this notice.

I have suspended your registration as a registered NDIS provider because I reasonably believe that
you have contravened sections 73V (the NDIS Code of Conduct), 73B (Requirement to be a
registered NDIS provider) and 73J (compliance with conditions of your registration) of the NDIS Act,
as provided for by section 73N(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.

The suspension means your registration ceases to have effect until 19 November 2022. The reasons
for my decision to suspend your registration are attached to this notice (Annexure A).

NDIS Provider Register

Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register
which must include certain information for each person who is a registered NDIS provider. This
includes if the registration of a person is suspended, information about the suspension. As I have
decided to suspend your registration with immediate effect, this information will be included on the
NDIS Provider Register, and published online.

Review under section 100 of the NDIS Act

You may request a review of my decision to suspend your registration within 3 months from the
date that the suspension takes effect.

Any application you may make for an internal review would be dealt with by someone other than
me, the original decision-maker.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                1
                                                185
                                                                                          Annexure AT9.5


Reasons for the decision to suspend your registration

Legislative provisions

Section 73J of the NDIS Act provides that registered NDIS providers must comply with conditions of
registration. The conditions they are subject to include those provided at section 73F of the NDIS
Act.

Section 73F of the NDIS Act states that the registration of a person as a registered NDIS provider is
subject to a number of conditions. These conditions include:

    •       Section 73F(2)(b) - a condition that the person comply with all applicable requirements of
            the NDIS Code of Conduct;
    •       Section 73F(2)(c) - a condition that the person comply with all applicable standards and
            other requirements of the NDIS Practice Standards;

Section 73T(1) of the NDIS Act states that the National Disability Insurance Scheme rules may make
provision for or in relation to standards concerning the quality of supports or services to be provided
by registered NDIS providers.

NDIS Code of Conduct

The NDIS Code of Conduct is set out in section 6 of the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of
Conduct) Rules 2018 (Code of Conduct Rules). Relevantly, Code-covered persons must, when
providing supports or services to people with disability:

        •      act with respect for individual rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and
               decision-making in accordance with applicable laws and conventions (section 6(a));
        •      act with integrity, honesty and transparency (section 6(d));
        •      take all reasonable steps to prevent and respond to all forms of violence against, and
               exploitation, neglect and abuse of, people with disability (section 6(f)).

Practice Standards

Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is registered to provide the following classes of supports under the
National Disability Insurance Scheme (Provider Registration and Practice Standards) Rules 2018 (the
Practice Standards Rules):

    •       Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements
    •       Item 20 – Household tasks.

In order to remain registered for the above classes of supports, Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd must
comply with the Practice Standards outlined in Schedule 8 of the Practice Standards Rules.

Relevant facts and circumstances

I have considered the following matters that give rise to the suspension:

    1. Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is a registered NDIS provider, registration number 4-4GHG-
       1073, trading as ‘Forward Focused’ (ABN 98643878639).

    2. Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is registered to provide the following services:



                                     NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                              3
                                                   187
                                                                                                  T9.5
       •   Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements; and
       •   Item 20 – Household tasks.

3. It is alleged that Forward Focused Group is providing supports to participants that the
   provider is not registered to provide. The FFG website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au)
   in its ‘Service Offerings’ offers SDA (Specialist Disability Accommodation) at three suburb
   locations. The Forward Focus Group Facebook page posted on at least two occasions 18 July
   2022 and 28 September 2022 with the SDA service offering.
4. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the
   safety and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission also
   holds information that suggests that Forward Focused Group does not support individuals to
   raise any complaints they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation
   from Forward Focused Group or its directors and key personnel.
5. This information strongly indicates that Forward Focused Group does not have a complaints
   management and resolution system in place that ensures that complaints are welcomed,
   acknowledged, respected and well-managed.

6. The Commission holds information that Forward Focused Group does not employ workers
   who hold the relevant qualifications for their roles. The website
   (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in its ‘Our Team’ section states that Ms Sally See was
   employed by Forward Focused Group as a Counsellor. Ms See does not hold qualifications
   with either the Australian Counselling Association (www.theaca.net.au) or with the
   Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency (www.ahpra.gov.au). The Commission
   could not locate any records of contact information or worker screening conducted for Ms
   See.
7. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with respect for individual
   rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and decision-making in accordance with
   applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022, participants including Corina
   Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sort to amend or cease service agreements
   with the Provider. The written email engagement with the Provider indicated non-
   compliance with the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and
   included both veiled and direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,

       a. Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant
          funds and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant
          confirmed, in writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds
          from the existing manager the Founding Executive Director writes “we will be
          terminating your services with us effective immediately”.

       b. Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane
          Clulow advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the
          participant was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.

       c. On 1 July 2022, the Provider responds to an account statement from a supplier
          stating that, ““we will no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her
          contract with us.”

       d. Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham
          ceasing some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss
          their concerns. By email dated 19 June, the Provider through Vida Reid purports to
          cease support coordination and terminate the contract for all services with
          immediate effect. The email claims “ongoing abusive manner of our staff by yourself




                             NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               4
                                           188
                                                                                                     T9.5
            and the break in trust” as the grounds. The email includes a perceived threat that
            invoices are to be paid immediately or be reported to the NDIS.

       e. Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, provides
          that the support worker has a non compete and non disclosure contract which will
          prevent her for working privately for the participant. It also states that if the
          participant had “come to us in good faith…I would have said okay.”

       f.   Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, “I would
            like all of your outstanding invoices paid immediately please or I will report this to
            the NDIS”.

8. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with integrity, honesty and
   transparency.

   It is alleged that Forward Focused Group are knowingly submitting false claims for payments
   to the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly
   causing a loss to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The
   Commission is aware of questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.

   The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on
   dates known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider
   generated invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the
   necessary Plans or Services Agreements in place.

   The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
   show that the Founding Executive Director is withholding information from other staff of the
   Provider on potentially fraudulent invoices. By way of example,

       a. Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from a former employee to the Chief Financial
           Officer raising questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for
           participant that Forward Focused Group had not provided.
   The Commission holds records of correspondence between the Provider and participants
   which reflects a lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-
   payment or broken trust as grounds for the Provider to immediately terminate engagements
   with participants. By way of example,

       a. Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
          participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due
          to “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The
          Commission holds a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated
          27 March 2022, requesting that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the
          Provider cease.

9. The Commission holds information that the Provider has not taken all reasonable steps to
   prevent and respond to exploitation of people with disability.

   The Commission holds information that the Provider is seeking to act as both the service
   provider and the authorized person on behalf of a participant to engage with the NDIA in
   relation to support provision and funding decisions. By way of example,

       a. The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM
          Consent Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward



                              NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                 5
                                            189
                                                                                                  T9.5
            Focused staff member (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review
            of NDIA decisions and manage complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.

Concerns about contraventions of the NDIS Act

1. The nature of the information held by the Commission strongly suggests to me that the
   Provider has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the NDIS Act (as
   provided for by section 73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act).

2. The NDIS Act 73J requires provides to comply with registration conditions. It is a condition of
   registration that providers comply with the Rules. The National Disability Insurance Scheme
   (Provider Registration and Practice Standards) Rules 2018 (the Practice Standards Rules)
   section 7(1) requires providers to be registered to provide Specialist Disability
   Accommodation (SDA). The provider is clearly advertising the provision of supports that you
   are not registered to supply.

3. The allegations and information held by the Commission indicate a significant amount of
   false claims against the Scheme, that could have otherwise have been directed to fund
   essential supports and services to people with disability.

4. The Provider’s practice of billing multiple NDIS participants for supports and services not
   provided compromised the integrity of the Scheme and deprived these NDIS participants of
   receiving necessary supports and services that they relied upon and were entitled to receive.

5. The information available to the Commission regarding the way in which the Provider
   handles complaints also suggests that the Provider has failed its obligation under the NDIS
   Practice Standards to implement a complaints management and resolution system that
   ensures that complaints made by all parties are welcomed, acknowledged, respected and
   well-managed. My preliminary view, based on the information available to the Commission,
   is that Provider does not provide a supportive environment for any person who makes
   complaints.

6. The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and
   quality of care expected from NDIS providers when providing supports or services to people
   with disability.

7. The information before me in relation to the protracted length of time over which this
   alleged conduct took place and continued as well as the instances of similar misconduct
   against multiple NDIS participants suggests that Provider’s conduct was systemic in nature.

8. My preliminary view is that the need to protect the interests of people with disability, who
   may be particularly vulnerable, and ensure they are kept safe, outweighs the Provider’s
   interest in remaining registered in these circumstances. I am also of the view that allowing
   the Provider to remain registered in the circumstances would jeopardise public trust in the
   Scheme.

9. Having considered the functions of the Commissioner, the objects of the NDIS Act, and the
   need to consider the financial sustainability of the NDIS in exercising the Commissioner’s
   powers under the NDIS Act, I consider that the registration of the Provider should be
   suspended under section 73N(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.




                              NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                              6
                                            190
                                                                                                       T9.5
In deciding whether to suspend Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd registration, I have had regard to the
following matters as required by section 73N(4) of the NDIS Act:

a) With regards to the nature, significance and persistence of any contravention, or proposed
   contravention, of the NDIS Act (section 73N(4)(a))

       •   The conduct of Forward Focused Group towards NDIS participants is very serious and
           warrants action to ensure Forward Focused Group cannot continue to operate as a
           registered NDIS provider.
       •   The evidence available to the Commission strongly suggests that the provider is
           committing fraud by charging participants for services not provided, or excessive
           amounts. Based on information available to the Commission, it is highly likely that both
           Directors of Forward Focused Group (Ms Vida Reid and Mr Simon Reid) and the Chief
           Financial Officer (Mr Rejaul Alam) have committed the alleged fraudulent activities.
       •   Information held by the Commission also indicates that Forward Focused is providing
           supports and services that they are not registered to provide.
       •   The information provided by complainants also strongly indicate a pattern of bullying,
           harassment and intimidating behaviour displayed by the Founding Executive Director Ms
           Reid towards NDIS participants.


b) With regards to the action that can be taken to address any contravention, or proposed
   contravention (section 73N(4)(b)):

       •   Given the nature of the conduct described above and noting that it appears to be
           intentional and recurrent, the Commission considers it unlikely that the key personnel of
           Forward Focused Group will fully implement any corrective action prescribed by the
           Commission to address the provider’s contraventions.


c) With regards to the extent (if any) to which the person is conducting its affairs as a registered
   NDIS provider in a way that may cause harm to, or jeopardise, public trust in the NDIS (section
   73N(4)(c)):

       •   Based on the seriousness of Forward Focused and Vida Reid’s conduct and the potential
           impacts on NDIS participants, the Commission considers that there remains a significant
           risk to participants if Forward Focused Group was to continue providing supports to
           participants.


d) With regards to the health, safety or wellbeing of people with disability receiving supports or
   services from the person (section 73N(4)(d)):

       •   Forward Focused breaches of the NDIS Code of Conduct are particularly concerning. For
           the reasons detailed above, the Commission remains of the view there is an ongoing need
           to protect the interests, safety and wellbeing of vulnerable persons receiving disability
           supports and services.


e) There are no other circumstances considered relevant at this time (section 73N(4)(e)).




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               7
                                                191
                                                                                                     T9.5
Having regard to all of the matters set out above, I am satisfied that Forward Focus Group Pty Ltd
has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene, the NDIS Act (section 73N(1)(a)).

Taking into account the totality of the circumstances and the regulatory functions of the
Commissioner I have determined that it is reasonable and proportionate to suspend the registration
for a period of thirty (30) days under section 73N(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.

Opportunity to make submissions about a proposed revocation of registration under section
73P(4) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth)


I am a delegate of the Commissioner for the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
(the Commissioner).

Proposed revocation of registration and banning order

Section 73P of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth) (NDIS Act) gives me, as the
Commissioner’s delegate, the power to revoke the registration of a person as a registered NDIS
provider on the grounds listed in section 73P(1) of the NDIS Act. Relevantly, in relation to this
matter, I can revoke the registration of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd ACN 643 878 639 (“the
Provider”) if I am satisfied that it has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the
NDIS Act (as provided for by section 73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act).

A banning order, which can prohibit or restrict specified activities by an NDIS provider, if the
Commissioner has revoked the registration of the person as a registered NDIS provider (as provided
for by section 73ZN(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.

Opportunity to make submissions

Before deciding to revoke the registration of a registered NDIS provider, the Commissioner (or a
delegate of the Commissioner) must give the person an opportunity to make submissions on the
matter, in accordance with section 73P(4) of the NDIS Act. A separate submission process in relation
to any proposed banning order made under section 73ZN(1)(a) is not required as set out in section
73ZN(8)(b) of the NDIS Act.

The purpose of this letter is to inform the Provider of relevant facts and the matters that I have
considered in reaching the view the Provider has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to
contravene the NDIS Act and to give it the opportunity to make a submission about this before I
make a final decision.




                                    NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                             1
                                                  193
                                                                                                       T9.6

I have also formed the preliminary view that the Provider should be banned from providing NDIS-
funded supports or services to people with disability in the event that I decide to revoke the
Provider’s registration.

I will not make a final decision about whether to revoke the Provider’s registration as a registered
NDIS provider until the Provider has had an opportunity to make submissions to me. This gives the
Provider an opportunity to tell me anything that you, as a representative of the Provider, believe
may be appropriate to bring to my attention, to dispute any of the facts of this matter, to comment
on the seriousness of it or to put your view as to decision I propose to make. You may also wish to
make comment on the banning order I am proposing.

Relevant facts and information

As a result of information provided to the Commission and the Commission’s own enquiries,
I understand the following:

    1. Forward Focused Group is a registered NDIS provider, registration id 4-4GHG-1073, trading
       as ‘Forward Focused’ (ABN 98 643 878 639).

    2. Forward Focused Group is registered to provide the following services:

                Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements; and
                Item 20 – Household tasks.

    3. It is alleged that Forward Focused Group is providing supports to participants that the
       provider is not registered to provide. The FFG website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au)
       in its ‘Service Offerings’ offers SDA (Specialist Disability Accommodation) at three suburb
       locations. The Forward Focus Group Facebook page posted on at least two occasions 18 July
       2022 and 28 September 2022 with the SDA service offering.

    4. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the
       safety and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission also
       holds information that suggests that Forward Focused Group does not support individuals to
       raise any complaints they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation
       from Forward Focused Group or the key personnel.

    5. This information strongly indicates that Forward Focused Group does not have a complaints
       management and resolution system in place that ensures that complaints are welcomed,
       acknowledged, respected and well-managed.

    6. The Commission holds information that Forward Focused Group does not employ workers
       who hold the relevant qualifications for their roles. The FFG website
       (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in its ‘Our Team’ section states that Ms Sally See was
       employed by Forward Focused Group as a Counsellor. Ms See does not hold qualifications
       with either the Australian Counselling Association (www.theaca.net.au) or with the
       Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency (www.ahpra.gov.au). The Commission does
       hold any records of contact information or worker screening conducted for Ms See.

    7. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with respect for individual
       rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and decision-making in accordance with

NDIS Provider Register

Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register
which must include certain information for each person who is a registered NDIS provider. Relevant
to this matter, section 73ZS(5)(e) permits the NDIS Provider Register to include information about a
former NDIS provider, including information about the revocation of a person’s registration.

Should I proceed to determine to revoke the Provider’s registration and make a banning order,
information about the revocation and banning order will be included on the NDIS Provider Register,
available on the Commission’s website.

Making a submission

I have not made a final decision in respect of this matter and I will not until the Provider has had an
opportunity to make submissions in writing to me. This gives the Provider the opportunity to tell me
anything it believes may be appropriate to bring to my attention, to dispute any of the facts of this
matter or to comment on the seriousness of it.

Section 73P(4)(b) of the NDIS Act provides that the Provider may make submissions within 28 days
after receiving this notice. If no submissions are made within that time period, any revocation may
take effect as early as 7 days after the end of the 28 day-period. The Provider will be notified in
writing of any decision made in relation to the proposed revocation.

Any submissions the Provider may wish to make must be received by the Commission within 28 days
of its receipt of this notice unless it seeks, and is granted, an extension.

  Opportunity to make submissions about a proposed Banning Order under section 73ZN of the
                     National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth)


I am a delegate of the Commissioner for the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission (the
Commission).

Section 73ZN(2) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth) (NDIS Act) gives me, as the
Commissioner’s delegate, the power to make an order known as a Banning Order. A Banning Order
can prohibit or restrict a person who is or was employed or otherwise engaged by an NDIS provider
under the National Disability Insurance Scheme (NDIS) from engaging in specified activities.

Proposed Banning Order

I have formed the preliminary view that it may be appropriate in all the circumstances to make a
Banning Order prohibiting you for a period of ten (10) years from being involved in the provision of
supports or services to people with disability. However, should a banning order be made it will not
apply in relation to any supports or services that you may seek for yourself or that you may provide to
your son.

The basis for this preliminary view is that I reasonably believe you have contravened, are contravening,
or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, as referred to in section 73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act. Please
refer to Annexure A for more information.

Opportunity to make submissions

The purpose of this letter is to inform you of relevant facts and the matters I have considered in
reaching my preliminary view and to give you the opportunity to make a submission about this before
I make a final decision.

I will not make a final decision whether to make a Banning Order until you have had an opportunity
to make submissions to me. This gives you an opportunity to tell me anything that you believe may be
appropriate to bring to my attention, to dispute any of the facts of this matter, to comment on the
seriousness of it or to put your view as to the terms of any Banning Order that I may make.

Proposed Banning Order

The information available to the Commission indicates that you engage in dishonest and intimidating
conduct that falls below the standard expected from key personnel of a registered NDIS provider and
the director of a company that provides disability supports and services.

The need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be particularly vulnerable, and
ensure they are kept safe, outweighs your interest in continuing to be involved in the provision of
supports or services to people with a disability.

Therefore, my preliminary view is that I should make a Banning Order that prohibits you from being
involved in the provision of supports or services to people with disability for a period of ten (10) years,
on the basis that you have contravened, are contravening, or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, as
per section 73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act.

I am of the view that making a Banning Order against you would provide a strong level of protection
for people with disability and maintain community confidence in the Scheme.

In forming this preliminary view, I have placed significant weight on the following matters:

    •   The nature of your alleged breaches of the NDIS Act are serious, as they involve instances of
        verbal and emotional abuse involving threats towards vulnerable persons with disability to
        whom you were providing services and supports on a regular basis. The Commission holds
        information that suggested that you exerted undue influence over a number of participants
        that limited them from exercising their choice and control over their own supports and
        services;

    •   I am particularly concerned by recent reports to the Commission that indicate your breaches
        of the NDIS Act are still occurring. The Commission holds information that suggests that you
        have been using your position to exploit participants who are in vulnerable situations despite
        your knowledge of your obligations under the NDIS Act. For these reasons, I am concerned
        your breaches will continue to occur in future;

    •   I also hold concerns about your willingness to deliver substandard services to NDIS
        participants considering you and your child are also participants. This shows a distinct lack of
        empathy and care for people who share common challenges.

                                                 202
                                                                                                       T9.7

    •       The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and
            quality of care expected from key personnel of NDIS providers when providing supports or
            services to people with disability. The information before me in relation to the protracted
            length of time over which the alleged conduct took place suggests that your conduct was
            systemic in nature. Allowing you to continue providing supports or services to people with
            disability poses a continuing risk to people with disability.

For the avoidance of doubt, the proposed Banning Order will prevent you from having any
involvement whether directly or indirectly in the delivery, coordination and management of NDIS
funded supports and services to people with disability. The ban will therefore preclude you from
remaining a director and shareholder of Forward Focused Group while it remains an NDIS provider or
have any involvement in any other entity that provides supports and services to NDIS participants. For
example, the banning order will also preclude you from remaining as one of the listed officeholders of
the Hive Pty Ltd, Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd, Infinity Living Options Pty Ltd, Malibuhouse Pty Ltd or Exact
Financial Solutions Pty Ltd if they provide any supports or services to people with disability.

NDIS Provider Register

Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register which
includes certain information about NDIS providers and other persons.

Under section 73ZS(2A) of the NDIS Act, the NDIS Provider Register must include certain information
in relation to a person against whom a Banning Order is in force. The information that must be
included on the NDIS Provider Register is:

        •    The name of the person
        •    The person’s ABN (if any); and
        •    Information about the Banning Order.

Sections 17 and 18 of the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Provider Registration and Practice
Standards) Rules 2018 provides that the whole of the NDIS Provider Register may be published on the
NDIS Commission’s website unless:

        •    the Commissioner considers that the publication of part of the NDIS Provider Register would
             be contrary to the public interest; or

        •    the Commissioner considers that the publication of part of the NDIS Provider Register would
             be contrary to the interests of one or more persons with disability receiving supports or
             services.

If I decide to make a Banning Order against you, information about the Banning Order will be included
on the NDIS Provider Register and published on the NDIS Commission’s website.

Making a submission

I have not made a final decision in respect of this matter and I will not do so until you have had an
opportunity to make submissions in writing to me. This gives you the opportunity to tell me anything
you believe may be appropriate to bring to my attention, to dispute any of the facts of this matter or
to comment on the seriousness of it.




                                     NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                           5
                                                   203
                                                                                                       T9.7

Annexure A

NATIONAL DISABILITY INSURANCE SCHEME ACT 2013 - SECT 73ZN

Banning orders—NDIS providers

       (1) The Commissioner may, by written notice, make an order (a banning order) prohibiting
       or restricting specified activities by an NDIS provider, or by a person who was an NDIS
       provider, if:

           (a) the Commissioner has revoked the registration of the person as a registered NDIS
           provider; or

          (b) the Commissioner reasonably believes that:

                (i)     the person has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to contravene this
                        Act; or
                (ii)    the person has been involved in, or is likely to become involved in, a
                        contravention of this Act by another person; or
                (iii)    the person is not suitable to provide supports or services to people with
                        disability; or
                (iv)    in the case of an NDIS provider—there is an immediate danger to the
                        health, safety or wellbeing of a person with disability if the person
                        continues to be an NDIS provider; or

          (c) the person is convicted of an offence involving fraud or dishonesty; or

          (d) the person becomes an insolvent under administration.

           Note:      A person who is subject to a banning order cannot be registered as an NDIS
           provider in a way that is inconsistent with the order (see subsection 73E(3)).

Banning orders—persons employed or otherwise engaged by NDIS providers

      (2) The Commissioner may, by written notice, make an order (a banning order) prohibiting or
           restricting a person who is or was employed or otherwise engaged by an NDIS provider,
           or a person who is or was a member of the key personnel of an NDIS provider, from
           engaging in specified activities if:


          (a) the Commissioner reasonably believes that:
               (i) the person has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to contravene this Act; or
               (ii) the person has been involved in, or is likely to become involved in, a
                       contravention of this Act by another person; or
              (iii) the person is not suitable to be involved in the provision of supports or services
                       to people with disability; or
              (iv) there is an immediate danger to the health, safety or wellbeing of a person with
                       disability if the person continues to engage in the specified activities; or




                                NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               7
                                              205
                                                                                                    T9.7

            (b) the person is convicted of an offence involving fraud or dishonesty; or
            (c) the person becomes an insolvent under administration.

Application of banning orders

          (3) A banning order may:

                            (a) apply generally or may be of limited application; and

                            (b) be permanent or for a specified period.

       (4) However, a banning order cannot prohibit or restrict activities that a registered NDIS
            provider is registered to provide under 73E.

       (5) A banning order takes effect from the day specified in the order.

       (6) A banning order is not a legislative instrument.

Process

       (7) The Commissioner may only make a banning order against a person after giving the
            person an opportunity to make submissions to the Commissioner on the matter.

       (8) However, subsection (7) does not apply if the Commissioner’s grounds for making the
            banning order are, or include, the following:
           (a) that there is an immediate danger to the health, safety or wellbeing of a person with
                  disability;
           (b) that the Commissioner has revoked the registration of the person as a registered
                  NDIS provider.

       (9) The Commissioner must:
           (a) give the banning order to the person against whom the order is made; and
           (b) include in the banning order a statement of reasons for the order; and
           (c) if the banning order is against a person who is employed or otherwise engaged by an
                   NDIS provider—notify the NDIS provider of the order as soon as possible.

Civil penalty

      (10) A person contravenes this subsection if:
           (a) the person engages in conduct; and
           (b) the conduct breaches a banning order that has been made against the person.

                Civil Penalty:    1,000 penalty units.

Post-legal advice considerations of Evidence, information and considerations.

1. Summary of tip-Offs
   1.1 The NDIA received fifty-seven tip-offs between 3 February 2021 and 16 August
       2022, alleging:
      a. Serious allegations including intimidating and provoking participants.
      b. Claiming for services not provided.
      c. Directing staff to create false invoices.
      d. Overcharging for services.
      e. Misuse of funds – claiming for services from incorrect line items and supports
         not reasonable and necessary.
      f. Forgery – cutting and pasting participant/nominee signature onto service.
         agreements and forgery of a signature
      g. Bullying and harassment of participants and staff.
      h. Exhausting funds from expired plans.
      i. Participant neglect – required supports were not provided.

2. Company structure
   2.1 In an email dated 31 May 2022 (Att 15) the Founding Executive Director of
       Forward Focused Group advised that at the end of 2021 the executive team
       comprised of “Simon, Rejaul and I”. It includes information about new staff being
       brought in and an attached file to show the company future.
   2.2 The email is issued to “ffg.net.au and hybridhive.com.au” email addresses.
   2.3 The attached file (Att 16) shows Parent Company “Forward Focused Group” with
       linking arrows to four sub groups including: Forward Focused with I [heart] NDIS
       logo, and The Hive Support Services with “The Hive” logo.
   2.4 Based on this and additional information provided by staff about access to
       accounts of both Forward Focused and The Hive it is reasonable to conclude
       that the entities the Commission knows as Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (Att
       17), Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd (Att 18) form part of “Forward Focused Group”.
   2.5 The news section of the Forward Focused Group website includes a note about
       the expansion into employment services under the banner of “Workforce
       Australia Employment Services” which is included on the company subgroups
       (Att 16)
   2.6 The Forward Focused Group website links to a Facebook page (Att 33).
   2.7 Service Agreements for participant and provider “Forward Focused Group”
       (Att 41 and Att 46) includes no ABN, ACN or registration number.
   2.8 Emails of 23 June 2022 shows email from Teams Coordinator @hybridhive
       email address to both @hybridhive.com.au and @ffg.net.au staff including
       Rejaul Alam and Vida Reid.

   Given the information presented it is reasonable to conclude that Forward Focused
   Group (FFG) is a catch all term for the companies which include the known entities
   referred to as Forward Focused and The Hive.


3. Companies interrelated
The information available both publicly and provided to the Commission demonstrates
that Forward Focused Group (FFG) is a name-only entity led by Vida Reid, Simon Reid
and Rejaul Alam. The name-only entity has a number of businesses including the NDIS
registered Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd also known as “Forward Focused”, and the
non-registered entity Hybrid Hive also known as The Hive.

Dear Mr Hahn

Re: Residence at 25/19 Malibu Road, Safety Bay

We refer to the above referenced property and confirm we act on behalf of Forward Focused
Group Pty Ltd on instructions from Ms Vida Reid.

On 21 January 2022 you entered into a lodging agreement with our client, to occupy
premises located at 25/19 Malibu Road, Safety Bay.

The lodging agreement is part of the services that, as a NDIS provider, our client provides to
eligible participants.

The fortnightly lodging fee is totalled at $332.08.

The terms of the agreement provide that you are required to pay a fortnightly accommodation
fee of $262.08 and a utility fee of $70.00. Therefore, the total fee payable each fortnight is
$332.08.

Under clause 17.3 of the agreement, our client maintains the right to terminate the
agreement in a number of circumstances, including, but not limited to –

(a)    The condition of the participant changes to the extent that the participant no longer
       needs the providers services, or their needs can be met more appropriately by other
       services or care.

(b)    The participants needs can not me met at the premises.




              Liability Limited by a scheme approved under Professional Standards Legislation
                                              239
                                                                                                T9.12

(c)    The participant cannot be cared or within the services due to unmanageable
       occupational safety and health risk, which puts Forward Focused Group staff and / or
       Service providers at high/extreme risk of injury or harm to health. This may include
       non-compliance with safety equipment and or safe work practices.

(d)    The participant breached policies and procedures and accommodation rules.

(e)    …

Further, clause 17.4 provides that our client may terminate the agreement immediately and
without notice in cases of “serious breaches” which include.

(f)    The participant fails to pay any accommodation fees within fourteen (14) days of due
       date for any reason within the participants control.

(g)    The participant causes or threatens serious injury to a member of staff or another
       housemate.

(h)    The participant causes or threatens damage to the premises or any other property at
       the premises.

(i)    Should there be a restraining order in place affecting and involving other occupants
       and / or support staff.


We are instructed that on entering in the agreement with our client and taking up residence
at the property you informed Ms Vida Reid that you were not going to pay the fees, as you
preferred to spend the funds received through the NDIS on “other things”.

On being informed by Ms Reid that your funding was, firstly, subject to being utilised for your
care and living expenses, and secondly, that you had agreed to be bound by the terms of the
agreement, you commenced acting in an antisocial manner engaging in such conduct as
follows –

1.     verbally abusing people who attend the premises;

2.     verbally abusing your fellow lodgers;

3.     punching tables and other items within the premises; and

4.     falsely informing vulnerable residents that our client is acting contrary to their
       interests for your own means.

On 30 March 2022, your actions resulted in emergency services being called to the
premises, and a distressed resident requiring immediate assistance.

A number of people were adversely affected as a result of your conduct, including our client’s
employee, who was in attendance with her 6-month-old child, and your fellow boarders.

Unfortunately, your actions amount to serious misconduct, and you have breached the terms
of the agreement, and continue to breach the terms of the agreement, by failing to pay the
fortnightly lodging fee.

                                                                                              T9.12

We put you on notice that your conduct is not confined to the lodging agreement, and in law,
also amounts to potential criminal conduct and breach of contract.

While our client is keen to support and assist participants, the situation has simply become
untenable, and our client is left with no alternative other than to terminate your agreement.

While our client is entitled to effect immediate termination, Ms Reid is agreeable to providing
you with a reasonable amount of time to find a new residence and to vacate the property.

Therefore, you are to vacate the property within thirty (30) days from the date of this
correspondence, being, 30 April 2022.

We put you on notice that if you fail to vacate the property, our client will commence to take
the appropriate actions against you without further notice.

You have the right to seek independent legal advice regarding this correspondence and the
termination of your agreement with our client.

MANDURAH WA 6210

By email only: candice@petherickcottrell.com.au

Dear Ms Jonker

Re: Forward Focused Group; Incident 12 August 2022 and 17 August 2022

We confirm we have been retained by Forward Focused Group, on instructions from Ms Vida Reid.,
and have to hand your correspondence dated 16 August 2022.

A Meghan Goodwin has been a recipient of our client’s services since December 2020, including
supported independent living at one of our client’s properties in Safety Bay.

Ms Vida Reid holds the current appointment as Meghan’s legal plan nominee and next of kin, and we
enclose the relevant documentation for your review.

We are instructed that on Friday 12 August 2022, Meghan was covertly removed from the property
by an ex-employee of our client, who was posing as a support worker. As correctly stated in your
correspondence, Meghan is a vulnerable person, and our client, being responsible at the time for
Meghan, was understandably concerned for her welfare, and contacted the police.

Shortly after Meghan was covertly removed from her accommodation, our client lost all access to
Meghan’s plan management portal and received an email from Meghan’s personal email account,
which stated that she was ceasing all services from our client and their associated company, The
Hive.

Our client then received correspondence from a Ms Sukhi Tear, of Diversitas WA, who informed our
client that she was Meghan’s current support coordinator.

Prior to Meghan’s removal from her accommodation, our client had not been informed by your
client, or the relevant agencies, as to any changes with respect to her care services or provider. As we




                 Liability Limited by a scheme approved under Professional Standards Legislation
                                                242
                                                                                                    T9.13

are sure you will appreciate, there is a process to be followed, and said process does not justify the
covert removal of a vulnerable person from safe accommodation at night.

Whether or not Meghan has freely elected to cease receiving services with our client is irrelevant.
Correct procedure was not followed, and at the time Meghan was taken from her supported
independent living coordination, she was under the care and support of our client. Consequently, it is
our client that, at the time of Meghan’s covert removal, held a duty of care for Meghan, and was
therefore responsible for her safety and wellbeing.

Our client wholly rejects that they have harassed or pressured Meghan to return to them. We are
instructed that Meghan has directly contacted Ms Vida Reid several times since her removal and has
informed her that she is scared and that she does not know where she is or what is happening.

Our client takes no issue with participants electing to move to another service provider. What they
take issue with is the proper channels of communication being bypassed.

Participants are not “clients”. They are vulnerable people in need, receiving a service, and due care
and process must be taken with respect to them, and the supports they receive. In particular,
Meghan, having a highly psychosocial support need, should not have been removed in the manner
she was.

Further, as the appropriate channels of communication have been disregarded by your client and
other involved parties, there is no way of knowing whether Meghan has left of her own accord, and
whether she herself sent the email requesting our client cease their services.

No relevant documentation or authorities have been provided to confirm the change in Meghan’s
services and provider. Given the absence of such, our client naturally holds grave concerns for
Meghan’s wellbeing.

Communication regarding any changes with respect to Meghan’s care services or provider should
have been provided to our client, with proper procedure followed, prior to Meghan being removed
from our client’s care.

Unfortunately, this is now the third time your client has removed participants from our client without
any communication. It is disappointing that your client continually disregards correct procedure and
we put your client on notice that we have informed the NDIS of same.

We are further instructed that earlier today, one of our client’s support workers, Madeline Dawson,
was approached and physically assaulted by the director of your client, Amanda Damon.

At the time of the assault, Ms Dawson was in attendance with Mr Shapira, an elderly participant,
who has recently undergone heart surgery and is recovering from a stroke.

We are instructed that Ms Dawson has reported the incident to the police and provided a statement,
and that Mr Shapiera has lodged a formal complaint with the NDIS.

Given the recent events, we are instructed to request that your client, and any associated parties,
cease and desist approaching or otherwise communicating with our client’s employees, officers, or
participants.




                                                                                               Page | 2

                                                243
                                                                                                 T9.13

Moving forward, any communication with respect to participants and a potential change in their
service provider must be done through the appropriate channels, with all involved parties duly
informed.

Yours sincerely




Rachel Robson
DFG Legal WA
Associate

                    STATEMENT OF MATERIAL FACTS
      PURSUANT TO SECTION 35 OF THE CRIMINAL PROCEDURE ACT 2004


Prepared by:
National Disability Insurance Agency                  DPP Telephone: 9264 7264
111 Eagle Street                                      DPP Reference: 2022PR01618
BRISBANE QLD 4000



OVERVIEW

1.     On 19 October 2020, the National Disability Insurance Agency (NDIA) approved
       Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, trading as Forward Focused (FFG), to provide services
       to National Disability Insurance Scheme (NDIS) participants in Western Australia. Mrs
       Vida REID (V REID) is the Director, shareholder and joint signatory to the company
       bank accounts. Mr Simon REID (S REID) is a shareholder, joint signatory to the
       company bank accounts and former Director.

2.     Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd (HYBRID) is an unregistered provider of services to NDIS
       participants in Western Australia. The sole Director of HYBRID is V REID. The sole
       shareholder of HYBRID is The HIVE Pty Ltd (HIVE). The sole Director of HIVE is S
       REID. The shareholders of HIVE are V REID and S REID. The joint signatories for
       the HYBRID bank account are V REID and S REID.

3.     It is alleged that, between 26 November 2021 and 22 June 2020, V REID and S REID
       caused 81 false ‘payment requests’ to be submitted against the funding of two disabled




                                         1035
                                                                                                      T45

      persons participating in the NDIS, with the intention of dishonestly causing a loss to the
      Commonwealth.

4.    As a result of these false claims, V REID and S REID received money reasonably
      suspected of being proceeds of crime to the sum of $247,484.78.

BACKGROUND

The NDIS, NDIS Participants and Participant Plans

5.    The NDIS is a Commonwealth Government Insurance Scheme established to provide
      funding to Australians with disabilities. This scheme is administratively managed by the
      National Disability Insurance Agency (NDIA). Persons assessed as being eligible to
      participate in the scheme each are given an individual identification number. Participants
      may represent themselves, in the case of children, a nominee or guardian may be
      appointed and in some cases a primary carer will act as the nominee or spokesperson for
      an adult participant.

6.    Participants of NDIS then negotiate with the NDIA to formulate a funding package to
      support the necessary and reasonable needs of the particular individual participant. This
      arrangement between the NDIS and the participant is documented and becomes the
      participant plan. Plans range in duration from three months to two years. In accordance
      with the wishes of the participant, the funds are either NDIS managed, plan managed by
      a nominated registered service provider or self-managed. Funds of self-managed plans
      are released directly to the individual and are out of the control of the Commonwealth.
      Monetary transactions on all other plans are managed through the Public Sector
      Collection and Disbursement System.

7.    The NDIA maintains a Customer Relationship Management (CRM) database that
      records participant plan data.

Service providers

8.    Participants are free to choose companies, businesses or individuals to provide the
      services they require. A facility is available through the NDIS for entities to become
      registered service providers. To achieve a certificate of registration, the applicant submits
      evidence to the Commission of their qualification /capabilities relevant to the services
      they wish to provide. A decision is then made whether or not to accept that entity as a
      registered provider of supports. Registered service providers are recorded on the NDIS
      webpage to assist NDIS participants to locate services and supports. Responsibility for
      the registration of service providers to operate in WA was held by the NDIA until 30




                                           1036
                                                                                                 T45

      November 2020. On 1 December 2020, the provider registration process became the
      responsibility of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission (Commission).

9.    Participants are free to choose unregistered service providers if their funding is “plan
      managed”. The provisions for “plan managed” funds are contained in Division 3 of the
      National Disability Insurance Act 2013. Unregistered providers must submit invoices to
      a registered provider who then submit claims directly to the NDIA. The NDIA pays
      funds to the registered provider who then forwards those funds to the unregistered
      provider.

Registration with NDIA

10.   On 28 August 2020, Forward Focussed Group Pty Ltd was registered as a private
      company limited by shares. At the time of registration, the principal place of business
      was recorded as 96 Amethyst Crescent Mount Richin WA.

11.   V REID has held the position of company Director from 28 August 2020 until now. S
      REID held the position of company Director from 28 August 2020 until 3 January 2021.

12.   On 9 November 2020, a declaration of suitability, purportedly signed by V REID, was
      uploaded to the NDIA systems.

13.   On 19 October 2020, Forward Focussed Group Pty Ltd was registered as a NDIS
      provider of supports for the provision of services in Western Australia.

14.   On about 19 October 2020, in the course of establishing itself as an NDIS provider,
      Forward Focussed Group Pty Ltd nominated ANZ Bank account numbered 016002
      640515184, bank account for the purposes of receiving payment from the NDIA. The
      sole signatory to this account is V REID.

15.   On 21 February 2021, Forward Focussed Group Pty Ltd nominated ANZ Bank account
      numbered 016253 152003884 (the FFG ANZ Account), held in the name S REID and
      V REID, for the purposes of receiving payments from the NDIA.

Registration with PRODA

16.   To submit claims for payment to the NDIA, registered service providers must gain access
      to the myPlace portal, which is a secure online system.

17.   To gain access to the portal, each user must create a Provider Digital Access (“PRODA”)
      account in the name of an individual, similar to a myGov account. This system provides
      a secure electronic means of identity verification for persons to engage with the
      Department’s Health Care system, including the NDIS.




                                          1037
                                                                                                   T45

PRODA Account in the name of MD Rejaul ALAM

18.   On 31 May 2020, at about 12.57pm, an account in the name of MD Rejaul ALAM was
      created in PRODA, with username: ‘alamreja’. (ALAM PRODA Account). To
      authenticate the identity of the person creating the account, the details of the following
      three documents were provided.

         •      Bank Card in the name of ALAM;

         •      Western Australia Drivers Licence no 7429948 in the name of ALAM; and

         •      Bangladesh Passport no BL0076718 in the name of ALAM.

19.   On 30 May 2022, the user ‘alamreja’ elected to receive PRODA verification codes via
      email ‘rejaul@ffg.net.au’. The PRODA account cannot be accessed without these codes
      being entered. Each time a person logs on, a unique code is sent.

20.   NDIA systems record that ALAM was nominated as an account manager for FFG
      between 27 October 2021 until 5 May 2022 and between 30 May 2022 until 4 October
      2022.

Unregistered NDIS providers

21.   On 17 June 2021, Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd was registered as a private company limited by
      shares.

22.   V REID has held the position of sole company Director and sole Secretary from 1
      December 2021 until now.

23.   The sole shareholder of HYBRID is HIVE. The sole Director of HIVE is S REID. The
      shareholders of HIVE are S REID and V REID.

INVESTIGATION

24.   Between 3 February 2021 and 18 April 2022, forty separate complaints were made to the
      NDIA in relation to the conduct and claiming patterns of FFG and HYBRID.

25.   On 3 May 2022, the NDIA placed a “compliance lock” on FFG. This means that all
      further “direct” claims submitted by FFG would be manually processed.

26.   Between 20 May 2022 and 1 September 2022, twenty nine separate complaints were
      made to the NDIA in relation to the conduct and claiming patterns of FFG and HYBRID.




                                          1038
                                                                                               T45

27.   On 1 September 2022 the NDIA commenced an investigation into the claims submitted
      by FFG and HYBRID.

28.   To date, twenty-seven witness statements have been taken with investigations ongoing.
      Further witness statements are expected and further forensic accounting analysis is
      required.

Registered NDIS Provider Assist Plan Managers

29.   ZETTAI Pty Ltd trading as Assist Plan Managers (ASSIST) is an NDIS registered
      provider that performs the services of a Plan Manager.

30.   At various times, ASSIST has received invoices from FFG and HYBRID for services
      purportedly provided to various NDIS participants. ASSIST has then made claims to the
      NDIA for these services, recieving funds from the NDIA, and then forwarding these to
      FFG or HYBRID.

31.   Karen MARASIGAN (MARASIGAN) is employed as the Head of Compliance at
      ASSIST and has provided a statement relating to invoices received from FFG and
      HYBRID, payment software utilised and remittance advices for payments. These
      remittance advices include the amounts of funds transferred to FFG and HYBRID and
      the account details.

32.   MARASIGAN further states that she met with V REID in January 2022 to discuss with
      her recurring errors in invoices being submitted to ASSIST.

33.   Michael PERIERA (PERIERA) is employed as the Senior Head of Service Delivery at
      ASSIST since 13 June 2022. PERIERA has provided a statement relating to his duties
      at ASSIST and his role overseeing the client care management team.

34.   PERIERA further states that he is the only person who will submit fraud reports to the
      NDIA.

35.   NDIA systems record that between 26 August 2022 and 21 September 2022, PERIERA
      submitted 11 separate reports of suspicious invoices submitted by HYBRID and FFG.

V REID Background

36.   V REID was employed by the NDIA as a Local Area Coordinator between 19 March
      2020 and 8 October 2020. As a former employee of the NDIA, V REID has knowledge
      of NDIA systems and processes. V REID is herself a participant.




                                         1039
                                                                                              T45

FFG Employee Statement

37.   Leah NORMAN (NORMAN) was employed by FFG from 14 December 2020 until
      October 2021. NORMAN also held shares in HIVE with V REID between 17 June 2021
      and 19 December 2021.

38.   NORMAN states that V REID encouraged her to inflate the amount of time she spent
      coordinating support services for participants.

39.   NORMAN also states that V REID would “invoice bomb”, meaning claiming for the
      residual funds left in a participants plan towards the end of the plan expiry. NORMAN
      states she fielded complaints from three separate plan managers about this type of
      claiming.

40.   FFG and HYBRID are responsible for $1,485,527 in claims from expired plans. This
      represents 31% of total successful claims.

OFFENCES

Charge 1 – s135.1(3) General dishonesty – Causing a loss

False claims for service to NDIS Participant Meghan GOODWIN

41.   On 30 November 2021, the NDIA issued a new plan for Participant GOODWIN
      (GOODWIN). This occurred because of an administrative error in the plan issued on 15
      October 2021.

42.   On 30 November 2021, CRM records that at 14:57pm, V REID contacted the NDIA
      requesting a copy of the new plan for Participant GOODWIN. This plan was emailed to
      V REID by an NDIA employee.

43.   On 30 November 2021, CRM records that at 15:24pm, three separate claims for payment
      were submitted using the ALAM PRODA Account, purportedly provided to
      GOODWIN by FFG. These claims were submitted against the plan expiring on 30
      November 2021.

44.   The claims submitted include 398 hours of house cleaning between 15 October 2021 and
      30 November 2021. This equates to 8.46 hours of cleaning everyday for 47 days. The
      claims also include 225 hours of House and Yard Maintenance between 15 October 2021
      and 30 November 2021. This equates to 4.8 hours of maintenance everyday for 47 days.




                                          1040
                                                                                                T45

45.     On 1 December 2021, the NDIA made a payment of $43,004.60 into the FFG ANZ
        account. Bank records obtained from Australia and New Zealand Banking Corporation
        show that the joint signatories for the FFG ANZ Account are V REID and S REID.

  Opportunity to make submissions about a proposed banning order under section 73ZN of the
                     National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth)

I am a delegate of the Commissioner for the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
(the Commission).

Section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth) (the NDIS Act) gives me, as
the Commissioner’s delegate, the power to make an order known as a banning order. A banning
order can prohibit or restrict a person from being involved in the provision of specified supports or
specified services to people with disability.

Proposed banning order
I have formed the view that it may be appropriate in all the circumstances to make a banning order
prohibiting you for a period of five (5) years from being involved in the provision of supports or
services to people with disability.

The basis for this view is that I reasonably believe that you are not suitable to be involved in
providing supports or services to people with disability. I am therefore empowered to make a
banning order against you under section 73ZN(2)(a)(iii) of the NDIS Act.

The purpose of this letter is to inform you of relevant facts and the matters that are of concern to
me and to give you the opportunity to make submissions about this before I make a final decision.

Relevant concerns
As a result of information provided to the Commission and the Commission’s own enquiries, I
understand the following:
    1. You are the sole director and shareholder of Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd and have been a director
       since 17 February 2022.
    2. On 19 October 2022, Ms Vida Reid (V REID), your wife and business partner, was arrested
       and charged with four offences, being:
            a. Between 1 December 2021 and 17 May 2022 at Perth in the State of Western
               Australia, V REID dealt with money or other property, it being reasonable to suspect
               that such money or other property was proceeds of crime and at the time of dealing
               the value of the money or property was more than $100,000, contrary to section
               400.9(1) of the Criminal Code (Cth).
            b. That between 26 December 2021 and 23 June 2022 at Perth in the State of Western
               Australia, V REID dealt with money or other property, it being reasonable to suspect



                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                1
                                               1044
                                                                                                       T46

            that such money or other property was proceeds of crime and at the time of dealing
            the value of the money or property was more than $100,000, contrary to section
            400.9(1) of the Criminal Code (Cth).
        c. That between 30 November 2021 and 08 April 2022 at Perth in the State of Western
           Australia, V REID caused payment requests to be submitted, which he knew to be
           false, to the National Disability Insurance Agency (NDIA), a Commonwealth entity,
           with the intention of dishonestly causing a loss, contrary to section 135.1 (3) of the
           Criminal Code (Cth).
        d. That between 18 November 2021 and 19 April 2022 at Perth in the State of Western
           Australia, V REID caused payment requests to be submitted, which he knew to be
           false, to the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity, with the intention of dishonestly causing
           a loss, contrary to section 135.1 (3) of the Criminal Code (Cth).

                        least 15 hours per day’ and that once this had been achieved, staff would
                        receive 50% of the charge as a bonus.
Basis for making a banning order
Based on the matters listed above, my preliminary view is that the banning order should prohibit
you from being involved in the provision of services and supports, both directly and indirectly, to
people with disability for a period of five (5) years.

The conduct set out above, engaged in by you and Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd, an entity for which you have
authority or responsibility for (or significant influence over) planning, directing or controlling its
activities, strongly suggests that you are not suitable to provide supports or services to people with
disability for the following reasons:

    •   Section 3 of the NDIS Act contains the objects of the NDIS Act, which includes:

            o   supporting the independence and social and economic participation of people with
                disability;
            o   enabling people with disability to exercise choice and control in the pursuit of their
                goals and the planning and delivery of their supports;
            o   promoting the provision of high quality and innovative supports that enable people
                with disability to maximise independent lifestyles and full inclusion in the
                community; and
            o   protecting and prevent people with disability from experiencing harm arising from
                poor quality or unsafe supports or services provided under the NDIS.

    •   In giving effect to the objects of the NDIS Act:




                                   NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                7
                                                1050
                                                                                                            T46

            o   section 3(3)(b) of the NDIS Act requires regard to be had to the need to ensure the
                financial sustainability of the NDIS. Similarly, as set out in section 4(17) of the NDIS
                Act, it is the intention of Parliament that the Commissioner, amongst others, have
                regard to the need to ensure the financial sustainability of the NDIS in performing
                functions and exercising powers under the NDIS Act;

            o   sections 4(4) and 4(6) of the NDIS Act, provide principles guiding actions under the
                NDIS Act including:

                    ▪    People with disability should be supported to exercise choice, including in
                         relation to taking reasonable risks, in the pursuit of their goals and the
                         planning and delivery of their supports; and

                    ▪    People with disability have the same right as other members of Australian
                         society to respect for their worth and dignity and to live free from abuse,
                         neglect and exploitation.

    •   It is my view based on the conduct set out above that you and Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd, an entity
        for which you have responsibility and authority, have acted in a manner that is contrary to
        these guiding principles, as well as the objects of the NDIS Act, and in circumstances have
        deliberately undermined those principles. More specifically:

            o   Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd engaged in conduct which limited NDIS participants from
                exercising their choice and control over their own supports and services, and acted
                without their consent or authorisation to make decisions about the engagement of
                supports and services under their NDIS plans. This conduct is serious, and has had
                significant impacts on the health and wellbeing of participants, particularly where
                deliberate conduct has been engaged in to subvert the authority of NDIS participants
                and their nominees;

            o   Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd has engaged in aggressive and dishonest conduct, including the
                submission of falsified claims for supports and services against NDIS plans, driving a
                culture of inflating claims and hours for services delivered, authorising payments
                without participant consent and entering into new service agreements without the
                knowledge or consent of NDIS participants and their nominees; and

            o   The conduct set out above and the offences for which V REID has been charged and
                you involved, presents significant risks to the financial sustainability of the NDIS, as
                well as to NDIS participants, as victims of fraud. Such conduct takes funds from NDIS
                participant plans, stopping participants from being able to purchase the supports and
                service they genuinely need. Further, fraud makes it harder for participants to pursue
                their goals and outs additional pressures on carers, family, friends and support
                networks.

Therefore, I have formed the preliminary view that allowing you to continue to provide supports or
services to people with disability poses a continuing risk to people with disability. My view is that is
that the need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be particularly vulnerable,
and ensure that they are kept safe, requires that in matters of this kind, a person who has engaged
and been involved in the conduct set out above, must be prevented from delivering supports and
services to people with disability. My preliminary view is that you are not currently suitable to provide
supports or services to people with disability.

3. Extension of time

We note that the Commission relied on a number of materials and information in making its
decision that is yet to be disclosed to our client.

Given the extent and seriousness of the allegation against our client, coupled with the lack of
disclosure from the Commission, we seek a further 28 days extension from the date of disclosure.

4. Next steps

In order for us to provide a submission to respond to the Commission’s allegations, we seek the
following:

       a. disclosure of all relevant information and materials, including but not limited to, as
          indicated in part 2 of this letter; and

       b. a further 28 days extension of time from the date the Commission provides us with its
          disclosure.

Given that time is of the essence, we request a response to the above request within seven days
of this letter.

Please do not hesitate to contact us if you have any queries.

Kind regards


Tom & Co Legal
