Compliance and enforcement
Monitoring and compliance is important to the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission’s work as a
regulator. It is one of the ways that the NDIS Commission can encourage best practice among NDIS
providers and manage risk to NDIS participants.

The NDIS Commission works with NDIS providers and their workers to help them comply with quality
and safeguards requirements, including through education and training about their obligations.

The NDIS Commission monitors registered providers for compliance with the conditions of their
registration. Most registered providers must undergo a periodic audit that assesses their
performance against the NDIS Practice Standards.

By knowing more about what happens in the NDIS marketplace, the NDIS Commission can work to
support ongoing improvement.

The NDIS Commission also investigates complaints and reports of non-compliance with the NDIS
Practice Standards, NDIS Code of Conduct and other quality and safeguards requirements where
they apply. This includes unregistered providers’ compliance with the NDIS Code of Conduct.

The NDIS Commission has the power to investigate any matters relating to registered and
unregistered providers and workers. It can impose penalties, including, in the most serious cases,
banning workers, de-registering providers and seeking civil penalties.

In determining its response to non-compliance, the NDIS Commission and Commissioner considers a
range of factors including:

• the impact of non-compliance on the person with disability, and the broader community;

• whether there is any immediate or ongoing risk to the well-being of the NDIS participant;

• the seriousness of any non-compliance and the actual or potential harm or consequences;

• how far below acceptable standards the conduct falls and the extent to which the person
  contributed to the risk, including whether it was intentional, reckless, negligent or a mistake; and

• potential to return the provider to full compliance, and as soon as possible.


Regulatory framework
The NDIS Commission’s regulatory powers and functions are set out in the National Disability
Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (the Act) and associated Rules.

These functions are developmental, preventive and corrective, and target individuals, the workforce
and providers to:

• strengthen and build capacity;

• prevent harm and improve the quality of services; and

• resolve problems and provide oversight.




                                   NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                            4
                                                1059
                                                                                                          T48

Non-compliance may be handled using a mix of tools including education, capacity building and
development for people with disability (and their NDIS providers and workers), complaints handling,
compliance and enforcement functions and related powers.

The NDIS Commission will take a responsive and proportionate approach to regulation, applying the
strongest actions to the most serious issues and breaches.


Human Rights
The NDIS Commission undertakes its regulatory functions in accordance with the Act and with due
regard to the United Nations Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities (the Convention).

The NDIS Commission aims to uphold the rights of people with disability, as part of Australia’s
commitment to the Convention, to prevent exploitation, violence and abuse of people with
disability. The NDIS Commission achieves this through:

• balancing the need to provide appropriate protections, with the need to enable people with
  disability to exercise choice and control;

• focussing on building the capacity of people with disability, their families and carers to make
  informed decisions about NDIS providers and pursue concerns or complaints; and

• supporting a responsive and effective NDIS market for disability supports and services.


Procedural fairness
In exercising compliance, investigation and enforcement powers, the NDIS Commission adheres to
the requirements of procedural fairness which means:

• the Commissioner and staff of the NDIS Commission will avoid actual and perceived conflicts of
  interest and act in an impartial and objective manner; and

• affording a person an opportunity to be heard before making a decision affecting their interests.


Compliance and enforcement strategy
The NDIS Commission aims to achieve a balance between supporting people with disability to make
informed choices, while also promoting high quality supports and services with appropriate
safeguards to support the development of a responsive and effective NDIS market.

To achieve these objectives, the NDIS Commission uses the following integrated strategies:

• encourage compliance with the law by educating and informing providers, participants and
  others about their rights and responsibilities under the Act;

• work with other agencies to implement these strategies, including through coordinated
  approaches and appropriate referrals;

• analyse emerging risks to identify potential market risks to inform compliance and enforcement
  measures, and identify priorities for regulation; and




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                              5
                                               1060
                                                                                                           T48

• enforce the law, including through the use of administrative action and by initiating formal
  enforcement proceedings where that is necessary and appropriate.

If the NDIS Commission has concerns that an NDIS provider may not be complying with legislative
requirements, or may become unable to comply, the initial focus of the NDIS Commission’s
approach, where appropriate, is to engage with the NDIS provider to support compliance.

Relevant considerations for the NDIS Commission in determining what, if any, further actions are to
be taken will include: whether the provider has an understanding of the problem; whether the
provider demonstrates a willingness to act to address the problem; whether the provider has a
history of non-compliance; whether the non-compliance appears to be serious or systemic.


The compliance pyramid
A wide range of tools, methods and powers are available to the NDIS Commission. The actions to be
taken will be determined on a case by case basis, including:

• the seriousness of the issue;

• the appropriateness of the person’s response; and

• the likelihood of further harm.

The compliance and enforcement actions available to the NDIS Commission are:

• administrative (these actions include education, corrective action requests, warning letters,
   compliance notices, infringement notices, enforceable undertakings, varying, suspending or
   revoking registration, and bans); or

• court-based (these actions include injunctions, taking action to enforce an undertaking, and civil
   penalties).

Administrative measures may enable earlier resolution than court action, and may also lend
themselves to achievement of appropriate and timely outcomes that are in a participant’s and/or
provider’s interests, and/or the public interest.

The NDIS Commission has published a suite of policies on our website that detail the nature of each
compliance and enforcement action and the approach generally taken in respect of each type of
action.

Information gathering, inquiry and monitoring powers are used to help prevent people with
disability from experiencing harm arising from poor quality or unsafe supports or services.

A monitoring activity, such as a site visit or regular contact with people working in the NDIS market,
may assess compliance with the Act generally or may target particular responsibilities or obligations.
This helps the NDIS Commission to make decisions about any further regulatory actions or support
that may be necessary.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               6
                                               1061
                                                                                                                         T48

The Ayres & Braithwaite regulatory pyramid 1 helps to illustrate how the NDIS Commission works to
support participants’ choice and control and to support a responsive and effective NDIS market.

Figure 1: Compliance Pyramid




The pyramid helps to show how the NDIS Commission takes a responsive and proportionate
approach to regulation, applying the strongest actions to the most serious issues and breaches.
It also demonstrates the ability to escalate actions if an initial response does not achieve the
intended outcome.

Some actions or regulatory tools are alternatives, while others are used in combination. Using a
range of tools from the base of the pyramid may often achieve compliance fairly quickly without
needing to escalate to more serious enforcement action, if appropriate. However, it is also open to
the NDIS Commission to proceed directly with the strongest actions in a particular case.


Educate and persuade
The primary approach to achieving ongoing compliance and building the capacity of the sector is to
educate, advise and encourage NDIS providers and workers to identify and understand their
obligations and improve their practice.

Measures may include practical advice to improve outcomes, targeted education and outreach
activities, engaging with the regulated community at the earliest possible stage, and providing timely
information and advice through the NDIS Commission website, social media, and information
sessions.



1 I. Ayres and J. Braithwaite (1992) Responsive Regulation: Transcending the Deregulation Debate. New York: Oxford

University Press. http://johnbraithwaite.com/monographs/




                                        NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                       7
                                                      1062
                                                                                                        T48

Such measures help to:

• raise awareness of the benefits of compliance, and the various measures that will be taken to
  address non-compliance;

• remove barriers to compliance, such as lack of awareness about obligations, confusion with other
  regulators, or particular accessibility needs;

• promote the objects of the Act, NDIS Practice Standards and NDIS Code of Conduct;

• support a reduction in restrictive practices; and

• reduce the risk that people will inadvertently take action that constitutes a breach.

Having provided advice or guidance to achieve compliance and being satisfied a provider has taken
timely and satisfactory steps to remedy a breach, the NDIS Commission may decide to take no
further action.

If a provider is not making timely progress to rectify non-compliance the NDIS Commission will
consider what additional action may be required to ensure the provider meets their responsibilities.


Investigations
The NDIS Commission will work with NDIS providers to support compliance with quality and
safeguards requirements, including through education and training about their obligations.

The NDIS Commission may also investigate complaints and reports of non-compliance with the NDIS
Practice Standards, NDIS Code of Conduct and other legislative requirements. The NDIS Commission
can commence an own motion investigation into any matter relating to compliance by registered
and unregistered providers and workers.

The NDIS Commission can exercise monitoring investigation powers (under Part 2 of the Regulatory
Powers (Standard Provisions) Act 2014 (Regulatory Powers Act) to check compliance with the Act or
whether any information provided pursuant to the Act is correct. Broader investigation powers
under Part 3 of the Regulatory Powers Act can be used where there is a suspicion that there has
been non-compliance with any civil penalty or offence provision.

The NDIS Commission’s information gathering powers under section 55A of the Act can be utilised
regardless of whether or not there has been non-compliance in accordance with the functions of the
NDIS Commission.

An investigation is the process of seeking information about alleged, apparent or potential non-
compliance, and may support, precede or follow other action taken by the NDIS Commission.

NDIS Commission staff follow the Australian Government Investigations Standards and a range of
other statutory requirements and policies, including:

• Regulatory Powers Act

• Legal Services Directions under the Judiciary Act 1903

• Australian Public Service (APS) values and APS Code of Conduct.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                            8
                                               1063
                                                                                                       T48

Appendix
Accessible version of the compliance pyramid:
A pyramid diagram, with a list of actions for the most serious breaches to less serious issues:

1. Ban

2. Revoke registration

3. Civil penalties

4. Injunction, infringement notice, suspend registration

5. Compliance notice, enforecable undertaking, vary registration

6. Registration, audit, investigation, corrective action request, warning

7. Education, persuasion, compliance support.

a banning order upon, Forward Focused Group PL; and (ii) issue a banning order upon Ms Vida Reid. Both
notices were issued on 19 October 2022.

the reasons and information that gave rise to our intentions.

Secondly, your letter seeks an “extension of time”. I have considered your request for additional time. I have
decided to grant your Clients an additional fourteen (14) days from the original due date of 5:00pm (AWST)
on 16 November 2022. I will accept submissions made by your Clients on the proposed (i) revocation of
registration of, and then issuance of a banning order upon, Forward Focused Group PL; and (ii) issuance of a
banning order upon Ms Vida Reid, up until 5:00pm (AWST) on 30 November 2022.

As advised in the notices of intention, no decisions have yet been finalised. The Commission will closely
consider all submissions received prior to this new deadline but, consistent with our original notice, will
progress to making final decisions even if submissions are not provided.

In relation to the suspension of registration of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (4-4GHG-1073) this
commenced on 19 October 2022 (the date of the Notice). The current suspension is in effect until
19 November 2022. The provisions of the NDIS Act do not provide for submissions to be made upon a
decision to suspend the registration of a provider but, rather, make provision for a review of such a decision
under s.100 of that Act. I understand your client, Ms Reid, has lodged an application for such a review on
Friday 11 November 2022.

   Dear Mr Halton

   Allegations against Forward Focussed Group and Ms Vida Reid – Response Submission

   We refer to the matter above and to the correspondence dated 19 October 2022 in relation to the
   proposed revocation of registration and banning order (‘Notice’).

   We confirm that we act for Forward Focussed Group and Ms Vida Reid (‘Clients’) in this matter.

   We welcome and thank the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission (‘Commission’) for the
   opportunity to provide a submission with respect to our Client having contravened, is
   contravening, or is proposing to contravene the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013
   (‘NDIS ACT’).

   Unfortunately, we sought an extension of time and additional information from the Commission
   to respond. To date, the Commission has neither granted nor refused these requests.

   Given the seriousness and breadth of the allegations, the lack of a decision regarding our requests
   and the current deadline, our Clients have attempted to respond to each allegation in turn with
   the information they currently have.

   RESPONSE TO ALLEGATIONS
   Forward Focussed Group (FFG) is a registered NDIS provider, registration id 4-4GHG-1073 and
   is registered to provide the following:

        •     Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements; and
        •     Item 20 – Household tasks.

   1) Allegation 3. It is alleged that Forward Focused Group is providing supports to
      participants that the provider is not registered to provide. The FFG website
      (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in its ‘Service Offerings’ offers SDA (Specialist
      Disability Accommodation) at three suburb locations. The Forward Focus Group
      Facebook page posted on at least two occasions 18 July 2022 and 28 September 2022
      with the SDA service offering.

   Our Clients deny this allegation.

TOM & CO LEGAL PTY LTD (ACN 647 358 698) | PO Box Z5567 PERTH WA 6000 | w: www.tomandcolegal.com.au | e: info@tomandcolegal.com.au | t: +61 (08) 6255 5737
                                       Liability limited by a scheme approved under the Professional Standards Legislation


                                                                        1074
                                                                                                   2
                                                                                                        T55

Our clients have promoted SDA housing on their Facebook page and submitted this in the context
of its partnership arrangement with Yaran Property Group and Ability Housing. Both these
companies are SDA providers. For the avoidance of doubt, our Client does not provide SDA
services and stated that they could assist participants to attain specialist disability
accommodation. The arrangements were merely a business relationship between our client and
the primary SDA providers.

Furthermore, our Clients have a shareholding in Infinity Living Options Pty Ltd and, as such, refer
any inquiries regarding accommodation to this company. A review of any invoicing to
participants for accommodation will show that our Clients are not involved in providing
accommodation.

2) Allegation 4. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints
   regarding the safety and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The
   Commission also holds information that suggests that Forward Focused Group does not
   support individuals to raise any complaints they may have directly with the provider,
   without fear of retaliation from Forward Focused Group or the key personnel.

Unfortunately, the Commission has not provided further details about these allegations. Our
Clients do not know the allegations concern 30 individuals or a few persons who have made
several complaints. Our Clients do not know what the complaints concern, when they were made
and who made them, whether they are some against Ms Reid personally or only concern FFG.

Given the said lack of particulars, our Clients cannot respond to the first sentence of Allegation 4.
Please provide more information so that our Clients will have a genuine opportunity respond to
this allegation.

As to the second sentence of Allegation 4, the Commission has not provided the information it
holds. In event our Clients emphatically denies the allegation that they do ‘not support individuals
to raise any complaints they may have directly with the provider.’ Our clients have consistently
provided help and support to all their clients.

In particular, all FFG clients are provided with, and have access to, many supporting documents
in relation to raising complaints. These include, but not limited to:
    •   Client information booklet;
    •   Client Money and Property Policy;
    •   Governance Policy;
    •   Feedback and Complaints Form (this has a specific clause advising clients how to make a
        complaint to the NDIS Commission);
    •   Feedback and Complaints Policy;
    •   Advocacy or Support Person Request Form; and
    •   Provision of Supports Policy.
FFG has a dedicated email for complaints and feedback (feedback@ffg.net.au).

FFG's website also contains links to the Feedback and Complaints Policy and other methods of
making complaints, such as links to the Commission. Furthermore, staff are trained and comply
with the following:

    •   Feedback and Complaints Criteria for Complaint Manager or Incident Manager;
    •   Feedback and Complaints Assessment, Investigations and Resolution Considerations;




                                             1075
                                                                                              3
                                                                                                   T55

   •   Feedback and Complaints Procedural Fairness Considerations; and
   •   Feedback and Complaints Criteria for Complaint Manager or Incident Manager.
Our Clients are willing to provide these supporting documents if required.

3) Allegation 5. This information strongly indicates that Forward Focused Group does not
   have a complaints management and resolution system in place that ensures that
   complaints are welcomed, acknowledged, respected and well-managed.

FFG denies this allegation.

As mentioned above, our Client has an extensive suite of internal policies and supporting
documents that clearly articulate our client’s complaint management processes.

FFG’s Feedback and Complaints Management Policy sets out how feedback and complaints in
connection with the provision of disability support services are encouraged and managed. The
Policy is complemented by the Feedback and Complaints Form, criteria for determining a
Complaint Manager, Procedural Fairness considerations and the organisation’s approach to
conducting investigations and resolving complaints (assessment, investigation and resolution
considerations), and complaints register. The Policy sets out how FFG’s clients can make
complaints, the process for managing and resolving complaints, training of workers (in respect
of complaints management), record keeping and continuous improvement regarding complaints
management.

A copy of the Feedback and Complaints Management Policy and Feedback and Complaints Form
is distributed by FFG to each participant by email, in person or another accessible form as
requested by the relevant client.

Additionally, FFG provides a copy of the documents to family members and others that form part
of the client's support network. It is further submitted that FFG takes the time to assist and
support clients to understand how complaints can be made during their first meeting and
continually upon request.

Please note, electronic records of the above are retained by FFG and can be verified by the
Commission.

Moreover, FFG encourages feedback and complaints and assists participants in making
complaints.

All complaints are managed fairly, efficiently and effectively, and a satisfactory resolution is
provided within reasonable timeframes. It is our Clients’ practice to proactively provide a safe
environment for complainants to make a complaint or provide negative feedback without fear of
adverse consequences, retribution or loss of service as a result of making the complaint.

4) Allegation 6. The Commission holds information that Forward Focused Group does not
   employ workers who hold the relevant qualifications for their roles. The FFG website
   (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in its ‘Our Team’ section states that Ms Sally See
   was employed by Forward Focused Group as a Counsellor. Ms See does not hold
   qualifications with either the Australian Counselling Association (www.theaca.net.au)
   or with the Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency (www.ahpra.gov.au). The
   Commission does hold any records of contact information or worker screening
   conducted for Ms See.




                                           1076
                                                                                                   4
                                                                                                        T55

Our Clients deny this allegation.

We are instructed that all of FFG’s employees and contractors hold relevant qualifications and
experience and are trained to provide person-centred support to our clients.

We are further instructed that FFG retains its employment records and maintains the strict
requirements of its Human Resources Management Policy, including 100 points of ID and relevant
screening checks, namely criminal record, working with children checks, disability worker
exclusion checks and visa before the commencement of work.

As to Ms Sally See, our Clients instruct us that she has the following qualifications: a Master in
Sexology; a Master in Counselling Practice; and an Honours Degree in Psychology with
Counselling. These qualifications demonstrate that Ms See does hold relevant qualifications.

We are instructed that Ms See does not hold current qualifications with either the Australian
Counselling Association or with the Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency.
Nevertheless, the 2018 NDIS Registration Guidelines advise that registration with ACA (or
equivalent) is required to provide counselling services. Ms See is registered with the
Psychotherapy and Counselling Federation of Australia (PCFA). Our Clients submit that the PCFA
is an equivalent agency to the ACA.

We note that the Commission states: “The Commission does hold any records of contact information
or worker screening conducted for Ms See”. Can we please confirm the Commission does have
copies of the relevant information?

5) Allegation 7. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with
   respect for individual rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and
   decision-making in accordance with applicable laws and conventions. Across June and
   July 2022, participants including Corina Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott
   all sort to amend or cease service agreements with the Provider. The written email
   engagement with the Provider indicated non-compliance with the National Disability
   Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and included both veiled and direct
   threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,

    a) Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant
       funds and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant
       confirmed, in writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds
       from the existing manager the Founding Executive Director writes “we will be
       terminating your services with us effective immediately”.

Our Clients deny this allegation and note not the information that is the basis of the allegation has
been provided.

It appears that the Commission is relying on the statement “we will be terminating your services
with us effective immediately” to demonstrate a lack of respect for Alexsis Bott. However, it is
important to note that this statement has been narrowly interpreted and taken out of context.

As background, there was a disagreement between the Plan Manager Marc from Manage Mode
and FFG. In summary, FFG identified a conflict of interest. This was because Manage Mode were
providing the same services to Ms Bott as FFG.

                                                                                                        5

manager, and despite repeated requests with Marc from Manage Mode, the outstanding funds
were never released. As such, our Client had no option but to terminate their services.

This email reproduced below, was sent on 10 June 2022 and demonstrated the respectful way in
which this client was dealt with by FFG:
          Hi Alexis,

   integrity, honesty and transparency.

   It is alleged that Forward Focused Group are knowingly submitting false claims for

respectfully request that FFG’s fitness to hold an NDIS registration not be determined until after
the said proceedings are completed.

   The Commission holds records of correspondence between the Provider and

7) Allegation 9. The Commission holds information that the Provider has not taken all
   reasonable steps to prevent and respond to exploitation of people with disability.

   The Commission holds information that the Provider is seeking to act as both the
   service provider and the authorized person on behalf of a participant to engage with
   the NDIA in relation to support provision and funding decisions. By way of example,

   a) The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM
      Consent Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward
      Focused staff member (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request
      review of NDIA decisions and manage complaints made by participant Meghan
      Goodwin.

Our Clients deny that FFG has not taken all reasonable steps to prevent and respond to the
exploitation of people with disability.

In relation to Ms Goodwin, her consent was obtained quite some time ago. At the time, Meghan
Goodwin was homeless and living on the streets. Our Client paid for her accommodation, and she
requested our Client's assistance to obtain her Centrelink pension as she claimed: “she had fallen
through the cracks” of the system. This is when the authority was voluntarily given to our Client
to act as a third party. Meghan specifically requested our Client’s employee Terri Roberts to act
as her third-party representative.

FINAL SUBMISSIONS

It is noted that not all information relied upon by the Commission to support the allegation that
our Clients ‘has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the NDIS Act’ has been
provided. As such, and without the requested documents, it has been difficult to formulate a
comprehensive response to satisfy the Commission that our Client should not be banned from
holding an NDIS registration. It is also a denial of procedural fairness.

Notwithstanding the above, we have provided responses based on the limited information
supplied. As such, we submit that:
   1. our Client is supplying specialist disability accommodation to be further examined.

   2. our Client does not support individuals raising complaints that are untrue and should be
      discontinued.




                                            1083
                                                                                                11
                                                                                                      T55

   3. our Client does not have a complaints management, and the resolution system is factually
      incorrect and, as such, should be discontinued.

   4. Ms See was employed and did not have the relevant qualification to be examined further.
      It is submitted that membership in the Psychotherapy and Counselling Federation of
      Australia may qualify as an equivalent qualification to ACA under the 2018 NDIS
      Registration Guidelines.

   5. across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant funds
      and support for participant Alexsis Bott to be discontinued. The information we have
      provided demonstrates the statement relied on by the Commission has been narrowly
      interpreted and taken out of context and that our Client has acted appropriately in the
      circumstances.

   6. a series of emails between 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 [sic] regarding participant Sarah-
      Jane Clulow advising a four-week notice for service termination be re-stated to include
      the statements relied upon by the Commission in support of the allegation so that our
      Client can provide an informed response.
   7. the Provider's response to an account statement from a supplier stating that “we will no
      longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her contract with us” must be read in
      context, and the assumption made by the Commission should be further examined.

   8. related emails between 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina
      Abraham be discontinued as the allegation relates to another entity and not our Client.

   9. related to the email of 16 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham being
      reconsidered, and the full circumstances are examined in closer detail.

   10. claims by the Commission of false invoices be discontinued at this time until the outcome
       of the criminal matter is known. This would also be a procedurally fair outcome.

   11. relates to the Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022
       requesting that participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed
       property due to “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee” be
       discontinued based on the information we have provided to the Commission.

   12. relates to the ‘FM Consent Third Party in relation to participant Meghan Goodwin be
       reconsidered given the explanation provided.

Our Clients submit that the claims made by the Commission are narrowly viewed based on an
illogical and irrational conclusion that was based on biased and, in some, factually incorrect
information.

We reiterate that the criminal proceedings should be determined before the Commission invokes
a revocation of registration and banning order against our Client, and as such, it is our view that
the suspension of FFG’s registration should be continued as it is just and reasonable to do so.

Background
10. The Commission holds information to suggest that Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (REG ID 4-
    4GHG-1073) and the former Director Ms Vida Reid have contravened, are contravening or are
    proposing to contravene the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (the Act), by failing to
    comply with their obligations.
11. The information held by the Commission that indicate contraventions of the Act by Forward
    Focused Group and Ms Reid are held in PDMS record EC22-002000.
12. On 19 October 2022, you issued:
    12.1. Notice suspending the registration of the NDIS provider Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd
          (Forward Focused Group) for a period of thirty (30) days, with immediate effect
    12.2. Notice of Intention to revoke the registration and issue a Banning Order to Forward
          Focused Group
    12.3. Notice of Intention to issue a Banning Order to Vida Reid for a period of ten (10) years.
13. The Notices of Intention to revoke the registration and make Banning Orders against Forward
    Focused Group and Vida Reid provided 28 days for the Provider and Ms Reid to make
    submissions. The original due date as stated in the Notices was 16 November 2022.
14. On 19 October 2022, Ms Reid was charged at the Armadale Police Station and appeared in
    Armadale Magistrates Court of WA.
15. Ms Reid was charged with the following offences:
    15.1. Between 1 December 2021 and 17 May 2022 at Perth in the State of Western Australia,
          Vida REID dealt with money or other property, it being reasonable to suspect that such
          money or other property was proceeds of crime and at the time of dealing the value of the

         money or property was more than $100,000, contrary to section 400.9(1) of the Criminal

          Australia, Vida REID caused payment requests to be submitted, which he knew to be false,
          to the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity, with the intention of dishonestly causing a loss,
          contrary to section 135.1 (3) of the Criminal Code (Cth).
16. On 31 October 2022, the Commission received a letter by Tom & Co Legal advising that they act
    for Forward Focused Group and Vida Reid. The lawyer responsible is Tom Monks (“Mr Monks”),
    Principal Director.
17. The letter sought “disclosure of all relevant information and materials” from the Commission and
    a 28-day extension on the due date of 16 November 2022 for submissions.
18. On 15 November 2022, you replied via letter to Mr Monks advising that the Commission is
    satisfied that the notices of 19 October 2022 adequately disclosed the reasons and information
    that gave rise to the notices of intention. The letter also provided Forward Focused Group and
    Ms Reid with a fourteen (14) day extension on the due date for submissions to 30 November
    2022.
19. On 21 November 2022, you issued a Notice suspending the registration of Forward Focused
    Group for a further period of 30 days, with immediate effect. The current suspension of
    registration expires on 21 December 2022.
Compliance QLD Review of the Submission
20. On 30 November 2022, the Commission received a letter by Tom & Co Legal enclosing the
    submission to the notices of intention.
21. The submission provides a response to each of the relevant facts and information held by the
    Commission regarding the Provider and Ms Reid, as outlined in the notices.
22. Compliance QLD has undertaken a review of the submission. Compliance QLD’s analysis and
    findings are provided in Attachments 4, 5 and 6 for your consideration.
23. For the reasons set out in the documents provided, Compliance QLD considers that the
    information supplied in the submission fails to demonstrate that Forward Focused Group or Ms
    Vida Reid have complied with their requirements under the Act.
24. Compliance QLD recommends you, as the delegate, review and consider the information
    contained in the documents in making your decision.
Delegation
25. The Delegations instrument, No. 2 of 2022 dated 21 June 2022, and effective 22 August 2022,
    provides the Branch Head, North Central West with delegation against NDIS Act s73N, s73P, and
    s73ZN.
Legislative Obligations
26. Section 73J of the NDIS Act provides that,
                                             Official
                                                 1087                                        Page 3 of 5
                                                Official                                                        T56
         A person contravenes this section if the person:
            (a) is a registered NDIS provider; and
            (b) breaches a condition to which the registration of the person is subject.
        Civil penalty:     250 penalty units.
27. The conditions include those provided at section 73F of the NDIS Act. Section 73F of the NDIS Act
    states that the registration of a person as a registered NDIS provider is subject to a number of
    conditions. These conditions include:
28. Section 73F(2)(b) - a condition that the person comply with all applicable requirements of the
    NDIS Code of Conduct;
29. Section 73F(2)(c) - a condition that the person comply with all applicable standards and other
    requirements of the NDIS Practice Standards;
30. Section 73T(1) of the NDIS Act states that the National Disability Insurance Scheme rules may
    make provision for or in relation to standards concerning the quality of supports or services to be
    provided by registered NDIS providers.
31. Further, the NDIS Act s73V provides that NDIS providers and members of the key personnel of
    NDIS providers must comply with the NDIS Code of Conduct, and
        (3) A person contravenes this section if the person:
                 (a) is subject to a requirement under the NDIS Code of Conduct; and
                (b) fails to comply with the requirement.
        Civil penalty:     250 penalty units.
32. The NDIS Act s73N provides for the Suspension of registration on the Commission’s initiative, for
    a specified period (not longer than 30 days) where
    32.1. S73N (1)(a) the Commissioner reasonably believes that the person has contravened, is
          contravening, or is proposing to contravene, this Act; or…
    32.2. S73N (1)(e) the Commissioner is no longer satisfied that the key personnel of the person (if
          any) are suitable to be involved in the provision of supports or services to people with
          disability, having regard to any matters prescribed by the National Disability Insurance
          Scheme rules for the purposes of this paragraph.
33. The NDIS Act s73P provides that registration can be revoked if the same circumstances as
    articulated at 14.1 are met.
34. Further, the NDIS Act s73ZN provides that Banning orders can be given if the same circumstances
    as articulated at 14.1 arise.
35. Both Revocation of registration and Banning orders require a process to be followed whereby a
    notice is issued to the relevant party affording them an opportunity to make submissions on the
    matter.
Financial Implications
36. Nil – activities form part of normal operations.
Regulatory Impact
37. Revocation of the registration and ban of a provider operating in a central, city location.
Consultation
38. Internally, WA State Office.

Dear Director,

                  Notice of Revocation of your provider registration 4-4GHG-1073
               under section 73P of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013.


I refer to my letter to you dated 19 October 2022 advising you of the proposed revocation of the
registration of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd ACN 643 878 639 (“the Provider”) and the relevant
facts and matters that I have considered in reaching the view the Provider has contravened, is
contravening, or is likely to contravene the NDIS Act. I also gave you the opportunity to make
submissions about this before I made my final decision.

I acknowledge the submission sent by Mr Tom Monks, Principal Director, Tom & Co Legal Pty Ltd to
the Commission on 30 November 2022. The Commission has carefully considered the contents of
the submission to determine whether to revoke the registration of the Provider.

I inform you that the contents of your submission have not changed the relevant facts and
information held by the Commission in forming the preliminary view to revoke the registration of
the Provider.

As detailed in the letter of 19 October 2022, the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS
Act) gives me the power to revoke the registration of a person as a registered NDIS provider on the
grounds listed in section 73P(1) of the NDIS Act.

The decision
I have made a decision to revoke the registration of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, commencing
from the date of this notice. Reasons for my decision are outlined in Attachment A.

I have revoked the registration of the Provider because I reasonably believe that the Provider has
contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the NDIS Act, as provided for by section
73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act. Section 73ZN(8)(b) of the NDIS Act also gives me the power to make a
Banning Order if I have revoked the registration as an NDIS provider. Please refer to the separate
letter dated 20 December 2022 regarding the Banning Order.

The revocation means the registration of the Provider ceases to have effect from 5:00pm (AWST) on
Tuesday 20 December 2022.



                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               1
                                               1090
                                                                                                    T56.1

Review under section 100 of the NDIS Act
You may request a review of my decision to revoke the registration of the Provider within 3 months
from the date that the revocation takes effect.

Any application you may make for an internal review would be dealt with by someone other than
me, the original decision-maker.

Should you wish to request a review of my decision, you may make the request in writing and email
it to review@ndiscommission.gov.au or send it by post, addressed to:

        Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
        NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
        PO Box 2862
        BRISBANE QLD 4001

Alternatively, you may telephone my office on 1800 035 544 to request a review of the decision or
arrange to make the request in person.
Once any review of the decision has been finalised by the reviewer, you may also seek a further
review of the decision by the Administrative Appeals Tribunal as provided for under section 103 of
the NDIS Act.

Non-compliance with the revocation
Please note that engaging in conduct that breaches the revocation of registration may be a
contravention of section 73B of the NDIS Act, insofar as the NDIS rules may require that specified
classes of supports provided under a participant’s plans be provided only by NDIS providers who are
registered to provide those classes of supports. A contravention of section 73B attract a civil penalty
up to $55,500 for individuals.

NDIS Provider Register
Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register
which must include certain information for each person who is a registered NDIS provider. Relevant
to this matter, section 73ZS(5)(e) permits the NDIS Provider Register to include information about a
former NDIS provider, including information about the revocation of a person’s registration.
As I have decided to revoke the registration of the Provider with immediate effect, information
about the revocation will be included on the NDIS Provider Register, available on the Commission’s
website.

Philip Halton

                                               1091

                                                                                                   T56.1



            Statement of reasons for revoking the registration of
     Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd as a registered NDIS provider under
 section 73P of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS Act)

This is a statement of reasons for the revocation of registration for Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd,
under section 73P(1) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS Act).


Procedural steps
I refer to my letter dated 19 October 2022 advising of the proposed revocation of the registration of
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd ACN 643 878 639 (“the Provider”) and the relevant facts and matters
that I have considered in reaching the view the Provider has contravened, is contravening, or is likely
to contravene the NDIS Act.
I also gave the Provider the opportunity to make submissions about this due no later than 5.00pm
(AWST) on 16 November 2022.
I received a request for an extension to that timeframe and granted additional time for submissions
through to 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).

I acknowledge the submission sent by Mr Tom Monks, Principal Director, Tom & Co Legal Pty Ltd to
the Commission on 30 November 2022. The Commission has carefully considered the contents of
the submission to determine whether to revoke the registration of the Provider.

The relevant facts and information held by the Commission in forming the view to revoke the
registration and make a Banning Order against the Provider are set out below.

Relevant facts and information

1. Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is a registered NDIS provider, registration 4-4GHG-1073.

2. Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (the Provider) is registered to provide the following services:

            Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements; and
            Item 20 – Household tasks.

3. It is alleged that the Provider is providing supports to participants that the provider is not
   registered to provide. The Forward Focused Group website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au)
   in its ‘Service Offerings’ offered SDA (Specialist Disability Accommodation) at three suburb
   locations. The Forward Focused Group Facebook page posted on at least two occasions 18 July
   2022 and 28 September 2022 with the SDA service offering.

4. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the safety
   and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission also holds
   information that suggests that the Provider does not support individuals to raise any complaints
   they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation from the Provider or the key
   personnel.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               3
                                               1092
                                                                                            ATTACHMENT A

                                                                                                     T56.1


5. This information strongly indicates that the Provider does not have a complaints management
   and resolution system in place that ensures that complaints are welcomed, acknowledged,
   respected and well-managed.

6. The Commission holds information that the Provider does not employ workers who hold the
   relevant qualifications for their roles. The FFG website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in
   its ‘Our Team’ section stated that Ms Sally See was employed by Forward Focused Group as a
   Counsellor. Ms See does not hold qualifications with either the Australian Counselling
   Association (www.theaca.net.au) or with the Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency
   (www.ahpra.gov.au). The Commission does not hold any records of contact information or
   worker screening conducted for Ms See.

7. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with respect for individual
   rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and decision-making in accordance with
   applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022, participants including Corina
   Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sought to amend or cease service agreements
   with the Provider. The written email engagement with the Provider indicated non-compliance
   with the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and included both
   veiled and direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,

           a. Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant
              funds and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant
              confirmed, in writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds
              from the existing manager the Founding Executive Director writes “we will be
              terminating your services with us effective immediately”.

           b. Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane
              Clulow advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the
              participant was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.

           c. On 1 July 2022, the Provider responds to an account statement from a supplier
              stating that, “we will no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her
              contract with us.”

           d. Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham
              ceasing some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss
              their concerns. By email dated 19 June, the Provider through Vida Reid purports to
              cease support coordination and terminate the contract for all services with
              immediate effect. The email claims “ongoing abusive manner of our staff by yourself
              and the break in trust” as the grounds. The email includes a perceived threat that
              invoices are to be paid immediately or be reported to the NDIS.

           e. Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, provides
              that the support worker has a non-compete and non-disclosure contract which will
              prevent her for working privately for the participant. It also states that if the
              participant had “come to us in good faith…I would have said okay.”

           f.   Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, “I would
                like all of your outstanding invoices paid immediately please or I will report this to
                the NDIS”.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                 4
                                               1093
                                                                                           ATTACHMENT A

                                                                                                    T56.1


8. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with integrity, honesty and
   transparency.

9. It is alleged that Forward Focused Group knowingly submitted false claims for payments to the
   National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly causing a loss
   to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The Commission is aware of
   questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.

10. The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on dates
    known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider generated
    invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the necessary
    Plans or Services Agreements in place.

11. The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
    show that the Founding Executive Director is withholding information from other staff of the
    Provider on potentially fraudulent invoices. By way of example,

            a. Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from former employee to the Chief Financial
               Officer raising questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for
               participant that Forward Focused Group had not provided.

12. The Commission holds records of correspondence between the Provider and participants which
    reflects a lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-payment or
    broken trust as grounds for the Provider to immediately terminate engagements with
    participants. By way of example,

            a. Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
               participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due
               to “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The
               Commission holds a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated
               27 March 2022, requesting that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the
               Provider cease.

13. The Commission holds information that the Provider has not taken all reasonable steps to
    prevent and respond to exploitation of people with disability.

14. The Commission holds information that the Provider is seeking to act as both the service
    provider and the authorized person on behalf of a participant to engage with the NDIA in
    relation to support provision and funding decisions. By way of example,

            a. The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM
               Consent Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward
               Focused staff member (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review
               of NDIA decisions and manage complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.

15. The nature of the information held by the Commission strongly suggests to me that the Provider
    has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the NDIS Act (as provided for by
    section 73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act).

16. The NDIS Act s73J requires providers to comply with registration conditions. It is a condition of
    registration that providers comply with the Rules. The National Disability Insurance Scheme



                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                5
                                               1094
                                                                                           ATTACHMENT A

                                                                                                     T56.1


    (Provider Registration and Practice Standards) Rules 2018 (the Practice Standards Rules) section
    7(1) requires providers to be registered to provide Specialist Disability Accommodation (SDA).

17. The Provider is clearly advertising the provision of supports that you are not registered to
    supply.

18. The allegations and information held by the Commission indicate a significant amount of false
    claims against the Scheme, that could have otherwise have been directed to fund essential
    supports and services to people with disability.

19. The Provider’s practice of billing multiple NDIS participants for supports and services not
    provided compromised the integrity of the Scheme and deprived these NDIS participants of
    receiving necessary supports and services that they relied upon and were entitled to receive.

20. The information available to the Commission regarding the way in which the Provider handles
    complaints also suggests that the Provider has failed its obligation under the NDIS Practice
    Standards to implement a complaints management and resolution system that ensures that
    complaints made by all parties are welcomed, acknowledged, respected and well-managed. My
    view, based on the information available to the Commission, is that Provider does not provide a
    supportive environment for any person who makes complaints.

Submissions

21. The Commission received a submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).

22. Your submission notes that a request for further time and further information were not
    responded to however, the Commission did respond to this request. This is evidenced by the
    Commission’s acceptance of the submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission
    date).

23. Your submission responds to a number of facts and information we raised in the notice of
    intention to the provider dated 19 October 2022.

24. Your submission confirms that the Provider held relevant information in relation to the issues
    identified.

25. Your submission denies all allegations.

26. I have carefully considered the information provided in your submissions alongside previous
    information outlined in the letter dated 19 October 2022. I note your submission does not
    provide the Commission with additional evidence to support your position.

27. As part of my considerations I am obliged to consider the NDIS Act. Section 3 of the NDIS Act
    contains the objects of the NDIS Act, which includes:

            a. Supporting the independence and social and economic participation of people with
               disability.

            b. Enabling people with disability to exercise choice and control in the pursuit of their
               goals and the panning and delivery of their supports.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                6
                                               1095
                                                                                            ATTACHMENT A

                                                                                                     T56.1


            c. Protecting and preventing people with disability from experiencing harm arising
               from poor quality or unsafe supports or services provided under the National
               Disability Insurance Scheme.

28. In giving effect to the objects of the NDIS Act, including those outlined above, section 3(3)(b)
    requires regard to be had to the need to ensure financial sustainability of the National Disability
    Insurance Scheme (NDIS). Similarly, as set out in NDIS Act section 4(17) it is the intention of the
    Parliament that the Commissioner, amongst others, have regard to the need to ensure financial
    sustainability of the NDIS in performing functions and exercising powers under the NDIS Act.

29. As a registered NDIS provider, Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, elected to participate in a
    national scheme aimed at assisting those who have permanent and significant disability, and
    providing those participants with choice and control over their supports and services, and their
    selection of the providers of those supports and services.

30. My view is that the need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be
    particularly vulnerable, and ensure they are kept safe, outweighs the Provider’s interest in
    continuing to provide supports or services to people with disability.

31. The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and quality
    of care expected from NDIS providers when providing supports or services to people with
    disability.

32. The information before me in relation to the protracted length of time over which this conduct
    took place and continued as well as the instances of similar misconduct against multiple NDIS
    participants suggests that Provider’s conduct was systemic in nature.

33. I am also of the view that allowing the Provider to continue providing supports or services to
    people with disability would jeopardise public trust in the NDIS.

34. I consider the alleged conduct of the Provider is entirely relevant to my assessment of the
    Provider’s suitability to be involved in the provision of supports or services to NDIS participants,
    in considering the objects and principles of the NDIS Act. This is particularly the case as the
    parties impacted by the Provider’s actions are NDIS participants.

35. Having weighed all the evidence at hand I reasonably believe that the Provider, has contravened,
    is contravening, or is proposing to contravene, the Act.

36. I am therefore satisfied that the power to revoke the registration is available to me and that it is,
    in all of the circumstances, a risk responsive and proportionate response.

Delegate of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commissioner

Dear Director,


Notice of Banning Order under section 73ZN(1)(a) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act
                                 2013 with immediate effect.


I enclose a banning order made under section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act
2013 (NDIS Act). I have made the order on the basis that I have today revoked the registration of
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd ACN 643 878 639 (“the Provider”), in accordance with section
73ZN(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.
This banning order prohibits the Provider from being involved in the provision of supports or
services to people with disability for a period of ten (10) years.
You should refer to the enclosed document, which clearly set out the banning order and reasons for
my decision to make such an order.
Please note that the banning order takes effect on 5:30pm (AWST) on Tuesday 20 December 2022.

Review under section 100 of the NDIS Act
You may request a review of my decision to make a banning order. You may do so within three
months from the date the order is served on you.

Any application you may make for an internal review would be dealt with by someone other than
me, the original decision-maker.

Should you wish to request a review of my decision, you may make the request in writing and email
it to review@ndiscommission.gov.au or send it by post, addressed to:

        Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
        NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
        PO Box 2862
        BRISBANE QLD 4001

Alternatively, you may telephone my office on 1800 035 544 to request a review of the decision or
arrange to make the request in person.



                                 NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 1 of 8
                                              1097
                                                                                                     T56.2

Once any review of the decision has been finalised by the reviewer, you may also seek a further
review of the decision by the Administrative Appeals Tribunal as provided for under section 103 of
the NDIS Act.

In addition to applying for a review of this decision under section 100 as outlined above, you may
apply for a variation or revocation of the banning order under section 73ZO of the NDIS Act. The
application must be in writing and explain why you believe the banning order should be revoked or
varied. If you are requesting a variation, you should also provide details of the type of variation
requested.

Any decision made pursuant to an application under section 73ZO is itself a reviewable decision
under section 100.

Non-compliance with the banning order
Please note that engaging in conduct that breaches the banning order that has been made against
you is a contravention of section 73ZN(10) of the NDIS Act, for which there is a civil penalty of up to
$222,000 for an individual.

NDIS Provider Register

Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register which
includes certain information about NDIS providers and other persons. Relevant information about the
banning order will be included on the NDIS Provider Register and published on the NDIS Commission’s
website.

This is a statement of reasons for the making of a Banning Order on 20 December 2022 against
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, under section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act
2013 (NDIS Act).
Procedural steps
I refer to my letter dated 19 October 2022 advising of the proposed revocation of the registration of
Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd ACN 643 878 639 (“the Provider”) and the relevant facts and matters
that I have considered in reaching the view the Provider has contravened, is contravening, or is likely
to contravene the NDIS Act.
I also gave the Provider the opportunity to make submissions about this due no later than 5.00pm
(AWST) on 16 November 2022.
I received a request for an extension to that timeframe and granted additional time for submissions
through to 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).

I acknowledge the submission sent by Mr Tom Monks, Principal Director, Tom & Co Legal Pty Ltd to
the Commission on 30 November 2022. The Commission has carefully considered the contents of
the submission to determine whether to revoke the registration of the Provider.

As detailed in a letter of 20 December 2022, I have made a decision to revoke the registration of the
Provider, commencing from the date of the notice.

As I have revoked the registration of the Provider, I have made the decision to make a Banning Order
against the Provider, as provided for by section 73ZN(1)(a) of the NDIS Act.

The relevant facts and information held by the Commission in forming the view to revoke the
registration and make a Banning Order against the Provider are set out below.

Relevant facts and information

1. Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is a registered NDIS provider, registration 4-4GHG-1073.
2. Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (the Provider) is registered to provide the following services:
          Item 8 – Assistance with travel/transport arrangements; and
          Item 20 – Household tasks.

3. It is alleged that the Provider is providing supports to participants that the provider is not
   registered to provide. The Forward Focused Group website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au)
   in its ‘Service Offerings’ offered SDA (Specialist Disability Accommodation) at three suburb
   locations. The Forward Focused Group Facebook page posted on at least two occasions 18 July
   2022 and 28 September 2022 with the SDA service offering.
4. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the safety
   and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission also holds
   information that suggests that the Provider does not support individuals to raise any complaints
   they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation from the Provider or the key
   personnel.



                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 4 of 8
                                               1100
                                                                                             ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                      T56.2


5. This information strongly indicates that the Provider does not have a complaints management
    and resolution system in place that ensures that complaints are welcomed, acknowledged,
    respected and well-managed.
6. The Commission holds information that the Provider does not employ workers who hold the
    relevant qualifications for their roles. The FFG website (www.forwardfocusedgroup.com.au) in
    its ‘Our Team’ section stated that Ms Sally See was employed by Forward Focused Group as a
    Counsellor. Ms See does not hold qualifications with either the Australian Counselling
    Association (www.theaca.net.au) or with the Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency
    (www.ahpra.gov.au). The Commission does not hold any records of contact information or
    worker screening conducted for Ms See.
7. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with respect for individual
    rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and decision-making in accordance with
    applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022, participants including Corina
    Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sought to amend or cease service agreements
    with the Provider. The written email engagement with the Provider indicated non-compliance
    with the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and included both
    veiled and direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,
    a. Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant funds
         and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant confirmed, in
         writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds from the existing
         manager the Founding Executive Director writes “we will be terminating your services with
         us effective immediately”.
    b. Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane Clulow
         advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the participant
         was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.
    c. On 1 July 2022, the Provider responds to an account statement from a supplier stating that,
         “we will no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her contract with us.”
    d. Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham ceasing
         some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss their concerns. By
         email dated 19 June, the Provider through Vida Reid purports to cease support coordination
         and terminate the contract for all services with immediate effect. The email claims “ongoing
         abusive manner of our staff by yourself and the break in trust” as the grounds. The email
         includes a perceived threat that invoices are to be paid immediately or be reported to the
         NDIS.
    e. Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, provides that the
         support worker has a non-compete and non-disclosure contract which will prevent her for
         working privately for the participant. It also states that if the participant had “come to us in
         good faith…I would have said okay.”
    f. Email 16 June 2022 by the Founding Director to participant, Ms Abraham, “I would like all of
         your outstanding invoices paid immediately please or I will report this to the NDIS”.
8. The Commission holds information that the Provider is not acting with integrity, honesty and
    transparency.
9. It is alleged that Forward Focused Group knowingly submitted false claims for payments to the
    National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly causing a loss
    to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The Commission is aware of
    questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.
10. The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on dates
    known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider generated


                                   NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                      Page 5 of 8
                                                1101
                                                                                         ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                  T56.2


    invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the necessary
    Plans or Services Agreements in place.
11. The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
    show that the Founding Executive Director is withholding information from other staff of the
    Provider on potentially fraudulent invoices. By way of example,
    a. Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from former employee to the Chief Financial Officer raising
        questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for participant that
        Forward Focused Group had not provided.
12. The Commission holds records of correspondence between the Provider and participants which
    reflects a lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-payment or
    broken trust as grounds for the Provider to immediately terminate engagements with
    participants. By way of example,
    a. Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
        participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due to
        “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The Commission holds
        a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated 27 March 2022, requesting
        that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the Provider cease.
13. The Commission holds information that the Provider has not taken all reasonable steps to
    prevent and respond to exploitation of people with disability.
14. The Commission holds information that the Provider is seeking to act as both the service
    provider and the authorized person on behalf of a participant to engage with the NDIA in
    relation to support provision and funding decisions. By way of example,
    a. The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM Consent
        Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward Focused staff member
        (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review of NDIA decisions and manage
        complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.
15. The nature of the information held by the Commission strongly suggests to me that the Provider
    has contravened, is contravening, or is proposing to contravene the NDIS Act (as provided for by
    section 73P(1)(a) of the NDIS Act).
16. The NDIS Act s73J requires providers to comply with registration conditions. It is a condition of
    registration that providers comply with the Rules. The National Disability Insurance Scheme
    (Provider Registration and Practice Standards) Rules 2018 (the Practice Standards Rules) section
    7(1) requires providers to be registered to provide Specialist Disability Accommodation (SDA).
17. The Provider is clearly advertising the provision of supports that you are not registered to
    supply.
18. The allegations and information held by the Commission indicate a significant amount of false
    claims against the Scheme, that could have otherwise have been directed to fund essential
    supports and services to people with disability.
19. The Provider’s practice of billing multiple NDIS participants for supports and services not
    provided compromised the integrity of the Scheme and deprived these NDIS participants of
    receiving necessary supports and services that they relied upon and were entitled to receive.
20. The information available to the Commission regarding the way in which the Provider handles
    complaints also suggests that the Provider has failed its obligation under the NDIS Practice
    Standards to implement a complaints management and resolution system that ensures that
    complaints made by all parties are welcomed, acknowledged, respected and well-managed. My
    view, based on the information available to the Commission, is that Provider does not provide a
    supportive environment for any person who makes complaints.
