                                              1102
                                                                                           ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                    T56.2


Submissions
21. The Commission received a submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).
22. Your submission notes that a request for further time and further information were not
    responded to however, the Commission did respond to this request. This is evidenced by the
    Commission’s acceptance of the submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission
    date).
23. Your submission responds to a number of facts and information we raised in the notice of
    intention to the provider dated 19 October 2022.
24. Your submission confirms that the Provider held relevant information in relation to the issues
    identified.
25. Your submission denies all allegations.
26. I have carefully considered the information provided in your submissions alongside previous
    information outlined in the letter dated 19 October 2022. I note your submission does not
    provide the Commission with additional evidence to support your position.
27. As part of my considerations I am obliged to consider the NDIS Act. Section 3 of the NDIS Act
    contains the objects of the NDIS Act, which includes:
             a. Supporting the independence and social and economic participation of people with
                 disability.
             b. Enabling people with disability to exercise choice and control in the pursuit of their
                 goals and the panning and delivery of their supports.
             c. Protecting and preventing people with disability from experiencing harm arising
                 from poor quality or unsafe supports or services provided under the National
                 Disability Insurance Scheme.
28. In giving effect to the objects of the NDIS Act, including those outlined above, section 3(3)(b)
    requires regard to be had to the need to ensure financial sustainability of the National Disability
    Insurance Scheme (NDIS). Similarly, as set out in NDIS Act section 4(17) it is the intention of the
    Parliament that the Commissioner, amongst others, have regard to the need to ensure financial
    sustainability of the NDIS in performing functions and exercising powers under the NDIS Act.

29. As a registered NDIS provider, Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, elected to participate in a
    national scheme aimed at assisting those who have permanent and significant disability, and
    providing those participants with choice and control over their supports and services, and their
    selection of the providers of those supports and services.

30. My view is that the need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be
    particularly vulnerable, and ensure they are kept safe, outweighs the Provider’s interest in
    continuing to provide supports or services to people with disability.

31. The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and quality
    of care expected from NDIS providers when providing supports or services to people with
    disability.
32. The information before me in relation to the protracted length of time over which this conduct
    took place and continued as well as the instances of similar misconduct against multiple NDIS
    participants suggests that Provider’s conduct was systemic in nature.
33. I am also of the view that allowing the Provider to continue providing supports or services to
    people with disability would jeopardise public trust in the NDIS.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                     Page 7 of 8
                                               1103
                                                                                            ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                     T56.2


34. I consider the alleged conduct of the Provider is entirely relevant to my assessment of the
    Provider’s suitability to be involved in the provision of supports or services to NDIS participants,
    in considering the objects and principles of the NDIS Act. This is particularly the case as the
    parties impacted by the Provider’s actions are NDIS participants.
35. Having weighed all the evidence at hand I reasonably believe that the Provider, has contravened,
    is contravening, or is proposing to contravene, the Act.
36. I am therefore satisfied that the power to revoke the registration is available to me and that it is,
    in all of the circumstances, a risk responsive and proportionate response. I am therefore also
    satisfied that the power to issue a Banning Order is available to me.
37. For the avoidance of doubt, the Banning Order will prevent the Provider from having any
    involvement whether directly or indirectly in the delivery, coordination and management of
    NDIS-funded supports and services to people with disability.
38. I consider that a timeframe of 10 years is appropriate and will provide a strong level of
    protection for people with disability.




Philip Halton
Branch Head, Operations – North Central West
Delegate of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commissioner

20 December 2022

Dear Ms Reid,
Notice of Banning Order under section 73ZN(2) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act
2013 with immediate effect.
I enclose a banning order made under section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act
2013 (NDIS Act). I have made the order on the basis that I reasonably believe that you have
contravened, are contravening, or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, in accordance with section
73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act.
This banning order prohibits you from being involved in the provision of supports or services to
people with disability for a period of five (5) years.
This banning order will not apply in relation to any supports or services that you may seek for
yourself or that you may provide to your son.
You should refer to the enclosed document, which clearly set out the banning order and reasons for
my decision to make such an order.
Please note that the banning order takes effect on Tuesday 20 December 2022 at 5:30pm (AWST).


Review under section 100 of the NDIS Act
You may request a review of my decision to make a banning order. You may do so within three
months from the date the order is served on you.

Any application you may make for an internal review would be dealt with by someone other than
me, the original decision-maker.

Should you wish to request a review of my decision, you may make the request in writing and email
it to review@ndiscommission.gov.au or send it by post, addressed to:

        Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
        NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
        PO Box 2862
        BRISBANE QLD 4001

Alternatively, you may telephone my office on 1800 035 544 to request a review of the decision or
arrange to make the request in person.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 1 of 8
                                               1105
                                                                                            ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                     T56.3

Once any review of the decision has been finalised by the reviewer, you may also seek a further
review of the decision by the Administrative Appeals Tribunal as provided for under section 103 of
the NDIS Act.

In addition to applying for a review of this decision under section 100 as outlined above, you may
apply for a variation or revocation of the banning order under section 73ZO of the NDIS Act.

Any decision made pursuant to an application under section 73ZO is itself a reviewable decision
under section 100. The application must be in writing and explain why you believe the banning order
should be revoked or varied. If you are requesting a variation, you should also provide details of the
type of variation requested.

Non-compliance with the banning order
Please note that engaging in conduct that breaches the banning order that has been made against
you is a contravention of section 73ZN(10) of the NDIS Act, for which there is a civil penalty of up to
$222,000 for an individual.


NDIS Provider Register
Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register which
includes certain information about NDIS providers and other persons. Relevant information about the
banning order will be included on the NDIS Provider Register and published on the NDIS Commission’s
website.
Yours sincerely




Philip Halton
Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
Delegate of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commissioner

                        NATIONAL DISABILITY INSURANCE SCHEME ACT 2013 (Cth)
                                       SECTION 73ZN
                          BANNING ORDER: STATEMENT OF REASONS

This is a statement of reasons for the making of a banning order on 20 December 2022 against
Ms Vida Reid, under section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS Act).

Reasons for my decision

1. At the relevant time Ms Reid was a Director, and key personnel of Forward Focused Group Pty
   Ltd (Forward Focused Group), Registration 4-4GHG-1073.

2. Ms Reid was a Director of Forward Focused Group until November 2022.

3. The Commission holds information that Ms Reid has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to
   contravene the NDIS Act.

4. On 19 October 2022, I issued you with a letter to inform you of the relevant facts and matters I
   have considered in reaching my preliminary view that it may be appropriate in all circumstances
   to make a Banning Order prohibiting you for a period of ten (10) years from being involved in the
   provision of supports or services to people with disability.

5. I also gave you the opportunity to make a submission before I made my final decision.

6. Submissions were due no later than 5.00pm (AWST) on 16 November 2022.

7. I received a request for an extension to that timeframe and granted additional time for
   submissions through to 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).

8. I acknowledge the submission sent by Mr Tom Monks, Principal Director, Tom & Co Legal Pty Ltd
   to the Commission on 30 November 2022. The Commission has carefully considered the
   contents of the submission to determine whether to make a Banning Order against you.

9. I note that you are no longer the Director of Forward Focused Group. With the exception of this
   fact, all other relevant facts and information held by the Commission in forming the preliminary
   view to make a Banning Order against you have not changed.

10. According to information available to the Commission, you as Director of Forward Focused
    Group, assigned yourself with authority to make decisions on behalf of NDIS participants. This
    information suggests that you were not taking all reasonable steps to prevent and respond to all
    forms of exploitation of people with disability. By way of example,
    a) The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM Consent
        Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward Focused staff member
        (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review of NDIA decisions and manage
        complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.

11. The Commission has received information and allegations that strongly suggest that you as
    Director of Forward Focused Group frequently engaged in bullying, harassment and intimidation
    behaviours directed at NDIS participants and Forward Focused Group staff. Your alleged conduct
    towards participants and staff has created an environment whereby participants and staff did
    not feel supported or safe to raise any concerns they have about the quality and safety of
    supports provided by Forward Focused Group.


                                 NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                      Page 4 of 7
                                              1108
                                                                                         ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                  T56.3

12. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the safety
    and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission holds information
    that suggests that you, as a representative of the Provider, did not support individuals to raise
    any complaints they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation from you.
    This information strongly indicates that you, in your capacity as Director and key personnel, did
    not ensure that complaints were welcomed, acknowledged, respected and well-managed.

13. The Commission also holds information that you as Director of Forward Focused Group were not
    acting with respect for individual rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and
    decision-making in accordance with applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022,
    participants including Corina Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sort to amend or
    cease service agreements with Forward Focused Group. Your email correspondence indicated
    non-compliance with the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and
    included both veiled and direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,
    a) Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant funds
        and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant confirmed, in
        writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds from the existing
        manager, you wrote “we will be terminating your services with us effective immediately”.
    b) Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane Clulow
        advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the participant
        was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.
    c) On 1 July 2022, you responded to an account statement from a supplier stating that, “we will
        no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her contract with us.”
    d) Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham ceasing
        some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss their concerns. By
        email dated 19 June, you purport to cease support coordination and terminate the contract
        for all services with immediate effect. The email claims “ongoing abusive manner of our staff
        by yourself and the break in trust” as the grounds. The email includes a perceived threat that
        invoices are to be paid immediately or be reported to the NDIS.
    e) Email 16 June 2022 sent by you to participant, Ms Abraham, provides that the support
        worker has a non-compete and non-disclosure contract which will prevent her for working
        privately for the participant. It also states that if the participant had “come to us in good
        faith…I would have said okay.”
    f) Email 16 June 2022 sent by you to participant, Ms Abraham, “I would like all of your
        outstanding invoices paid immediately please or I will report this to the NDIS”.
14. The Commission holds additional information that indicates that you played a central role in the
    alleged fraudulent activities engaged in by Forward Focused Group, in particular the submission
    of false claims for payments to the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme). I am of the
    view that this demonstrates a failure by you to act with integrity, honesty and transparency.

15. It is alleged that Forward Focused Group were knowingly submitting false claims for payments to
    the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly causing a
    loss to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The Commission is aware of
    questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.

16. The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on dates
    known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider generated
    invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the necessary
    Plans or Services Agreements in place.




                                 NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 5 of 7
                                              1109
                                                                                          ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                   T56.3

17. The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
    show that you were withholding information from other staff of the Provider on potentially
    fraudulent invoices. By way of example,
    a) Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from former employee to the Chief Financial Officer raising
        questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for participant that
        Forward Focused Group had not provided. In your response dated 9 May 2022, you replied
        “Whilst I appreciate your thoroughness in dealing with clients please remember that I do
        work on participants as well. I keep my work and my notes private as sometimes they may
        be about staff or complaints or other work clients have requested that they have advised
        not to be disclosed to other due to privacy and dignity … I am aware that you have recently
        gained access to some portals and kindly ask that you do not access these as they go against
        our policy and procedures.”

18. The Commission holds records of correspondence between you and participants which reflects a
    lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-payment or broken trust as
    grounds for you to immediately terminate engagements with participants. By way of example,

    a) Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
       participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due to
       “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The Commission holds
       a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated 27 March 2022, requesting
       that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the Provider cease.

19. The information available to the Commission indicates that you engage in dishonest and
    intimidating conduct that falls below the standard expected from key personnel of a registered
    NDIS provider and the director of a company that provides disability supports and services.

Submissions

20. The Commission received a submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).

21. Your submission notes that a request for further time and further information were not
    responded to however, the Commission did respond to this request. This is evidenced by the
    Commission’s acceptance of the submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission
    date).

22. Your submission responds to a number of facts and information we raised in the notice of
    intention to the provider dated 19 October 2022.

23. Your submission confirms that you held relevant information in relation to the issues identified.

24. Your submission denies all allegations.

25. I have carefully considered the information provided in your submissions alongside previous
    information outlined in the letter dated 19 October 2022. I note your submission does not
    provide the Commission with additional evidence to support your position.

26. Having weighed all the evidence at hand I have formed the reasonable belief that you have
    contravened, are contravening, or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, as per section
    73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 6 of 7
                                               1110
                                                                                           ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                    T56.3

27. As part of my considerations I am obliged to consider the NDIS Act. Section 3 of the NDIS Act
    contains the objects of the NDIS Act, which includes:

            a. Supporting the independence and social and economic participation of people with
               disability.

            b. Enabling people with disability to exercise choice and control in the pursuit of their
               goals and the panning and delivery of their supports.

            c. Protecting and preventing people with disability from experiencing harm arising
               from poor quality or unsafe supports or services provided under the National
               Disability Insurance Scheme.

28. In giving effect to the objects of the NDIS Act, including those outlined above, section 3(3)(b)
    requires regard to be had to the need to ensure financial sustainability of the National Disability
    Insurance Scheme (NDIS). Similarly, as set out in NDIS Act section 4(17) it is the intention of the
    Parliament that the Commissioner, amongst others, have regard to the need to ensure financial
    sustainability of the NDIS in performing functions and exercising powers under the NDIS Act.

29. The need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be particularly vulnerable,
    and ensure they are kept safe, outweighs your interest in continuing to be involved in the
    provision of supports or services to people with a disability.

30. In determining whether to make a Banning Order for you, I weighed the following competing
    interests and concerns:
        a. The nature of your alleged breaches of the NDIS Act are serious, as they involve
            instances of verbal and emotional abuse involving threats towards vulnerable persons
            with disability to whom you were providing services and supports on a regular basis. The
            Commission holds information that suggested that you exerted undue influence over a
            number of participants that limited them from exercising their choice and control over
            their own supports and services.
        b. The Commission holds information that suggests that you have been using your position
            to exploit participants who are in vulnerable situations despite your knowledge of your
            obligations under the NDIS Act. For these reasons, I am concerned your breaches will
            continue to occur in future.

31. The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and quality
    of care expected from key personnel of NDIS providers when providing supports or services to
    people with disability. The information before me in relation to the protracted length of time
    over which the alleged conduct took place suggests that your conduct was systemic in nature.
    Allowing you to continue providing supports or services to people with disability poses a
    continuing risk to people with disability.

32. I am of the view that making a Banning Order against you would provide a strong level of
    protection for people with disability and maintain community confidence in the Scheme.

33. For the avoidance of doubt, the Banning Order will prevent you from having any involvement
    whether directly or indirectly in the delivery, coordination and management of NDIS-funded
    supports and services to people with disability.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                     Page 7 of 7
                                               1111
                                                                                            ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                      T56.3

34. The ban will therefore preclude you from having any involvement in any other entity that
    provides supports and services to NDIS participants.

35. I accept that the making of a banning order for you will impact your ability to earn an income in
    the disability services sector, and I have taken this into account in my consideration to issue the
    banning order.

36. I consider your alleged conduct is entirely relevant to my assessment of your suitability to be
    involved in the provision of supports or services to NDIS participants, in considering the objects
    and principles of the NDIS Act. This is particularly the case as the parties impacted by your
    actions are NDIS participants.

37. I consider that a timeframe of five (5) years is appropriate and will provide a strong level of
    protection for people with disability.




Philip Halton

Dear Ms Reid,
Notice of Banning Order under section 73ZN(2) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act
2013 with immediate effect.
I enclose a banning order made under section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act
2013 (NDIS Act). I have made the order on the basis that I reasonably believe that you have
contravened, are contravening, or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, in accordance with section
73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act.
This banning order prohibits you from being involved in the provision of supports or services to
people with disability for a period of five (5) years.
This banning order will not apply in relation to any supports or services that you may seek for
yourself or that you may provide to your son.
You should refer to the enclosed document, which clearly set out the banning order and reasons for
my decision to make such an order.
Please note that the banning order takes effect on Tuesday 20 December 2022 at 5:30pm (AWST).


Review under section 100 of the NDIS Act
You may request a review of my decision to make a banning order. You may do so within three
months from the date the order is served on you.

Any application you may make for an internal review would be dealt with by someone other than
me, the original decision-maker.

Should you wish to request a review of my decision, you may make the request in writing and email
it to review@ndiscommission.gov.au or send it by post, addressed to:

        Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
        NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
        PO Box 2862
        BRISBANE QLD 4001

Alternatively, you may telephone my office on 1800 035 544 to request a review of the decision or
arrange to make the request in person.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 1 of 8
                                               1127
                                                                                            ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                            T57

Once any review of the decision has been finalised by the reviewer, you may also seek a further
review of the decision by the Administrative Appeals Tribunal as provided for under section 103 of
the NDIS Act.

In addition to applying for a review of this decision under section 100 as outlined above, you may
apply for a variation or revocation of the banning order under section 73ZO of the NDIS Act.

Any decision made pursuant to an application under section 73ZO is itself a reviewable decision
under section 100. The application must be in writing and explain why you believe the banning order
should be revoked or varied. If you are requesting a variation, you should also provide details of the
type of variation requested.

Non-compliance with the banning order
Please note that engaging in conduct that breaches the banning order that has been made against
you is a contravention of section 73ZN(10) of the NDIS Act, for which there is a civil penalty of up to
$222,000 for an individual.


NDIS Provider Register
Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register which
includes certain information about NDIS providers and other persons. Relevant information about the
banning order will be included on the NDIS Provider Register and published on the NDIS Commission’s
website.
Yours sincerely




Philip Halton
Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
Delegate of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commissioner

20 December 2022

                                       BANNING ORDER

I, Philip Halton, give this Banning Order to Vida Reid as I reasonably believe that, the person has
contravened, is contravening, or is likely to contravene this Act. (NDIS Act s73ZN(2)(a)(i)).

Pursuant to section 73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act, I prohibit Vida Reid from providing supports and
services, directly or indirectly, to NDIS-funded participants for a period of five (5) years from the
date that this order takes effect.

However, does banning order will not apply in relation to any supports or services that Vida Reid
may seek for herself or that she may provide to her son.

                        NATIONAL DISABILITY INSURANCE SCHEME ACT 2013 (Cth)
                                       SECTION 73ZN
                          BANNING ORDER: STATEMENT OF REASONS

This is a statement of reasons for the making of a banning order on 20 December 2022 against
Ms Vida Reid, under section 73ZN of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS Act).

Reasons for my decision

1. At the relevant time Ms Reid was a Director, and key personnel of Forward Focused Group Pty
   Ltd (Forward Focused Group), Registration 4-4GHG-1073.

2. Ms Reid was a Director of Forward Focused Group until November 2022.

3. The Commission holds information that Ms Reid has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to
   contravene the NDIS Act.

4. On 19 October 2022, I issued you with a letter to inform you of the relevant facts and matters I
   have considered in reaching my preliminary view that it may be appropriate in all circumstances
   to make a Banning Order prohibiting you for a period of ten (10) years from being involved in the
   provision of supports or services to people with disability.

5. I also gave you the opportunity to make a submission before I made my final decision.

6. Submissions were due no later than 5.00pm (AWST) on 16 November 2022.

7. I received a request for an extension to that timeframe and granted additional time for
   submissions through to 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).

8. I acknowledge the submission sent by Mr Tom Monks, Principal Director, Tom & Co Legal Pty Ltd
   to the Commission on 30 November 2022. The Commission has carefully considered the
   contents of the submission to determine whether to make a Banning Order against you.

9. I note that you are no longer the Director of Forward Focused Group. With the exception of this
   fact, all other relevant facts and information held by the Commission in forming the preliminary
   view to make a Banning Order against you have not changed.

10. According to information available to the Commission, you as Director of Forward Focused
    Group, assigned yourself with authority to make decisions on behalf of NDIS participants. This
    information suggests that you were not taking all reasonable steps to prevent and respond to all
    forms of exploitation of people with disability. By way of example,
    a) The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled ‘FM Consent
        Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward Focused staff member
        (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review of NDIA decisions and manage
        complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.

11. The Commission has received information and allegations that strongly suggest that you as
    Director of Forward Focused Group frequently engaged in bullying, harassment and intimidation
    behaviours directed at NDIS participants and Forward Focused Group staff. Your alleged conduct
    towards participants and staff has created an environment whereby participants and staff did
    not feel supported or safe to raise any concerns they have about the quality and safety of
    supports provided by Forward Focused Group.


                                 NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                      Page 4 of 7
                                              1130
                                                                                         ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                         T57

12. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the safety
    and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission holds information
    that suggests that you, as a representative of the Provider, did not support individuals to raise
    any complaints they may have directly with the provider, without fear of retaliation from you.
    This information strongly indicates that you, in your capacity as Director and key personnel, did
    not ensure that complaints were welcomed, acknowledged, respected and well-managed.

13. The Commission also holds information that you as Director of Forward Focused Group were not
    acting with respect for individual rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and
    decision-making in accordance with applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022,
    participants including Corina Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sort to amend or
    cease service agreements with Forward Focused Group. Your email correspondence indicated
    non-compliance with the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and
    included both veiled and direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,
    a) Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant funds
        and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant confirmed, in
        writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds from the existing
        manager, you wrote “we will be terminating your services with us effective immediately”.
    b) Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane Clulow
        advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the participant
        was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.
    c) On 1 July 2022, you responded to an account statement from a supplier stating that, “we will
        no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has terminated her contract with us.”
    d) Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham ceasing
        some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss their concerns. By
        email dated 19 June, you purport to cease support coordination and terminate the contract
        for all services with immediate effect. The email claims “ongoing abusive manner of our staff
        by yourself and the break in trust” as the grounds. The email includes a perceived threat that
        invoices are to be paid immediately or be reported to the NDIS.
    e) Email 16 June 2022 sent by you to participant, Ms Abraham, provides that the support
        worker has a non-compete and non-disclosure contract which will prevent her for working
        privately for the participant. It also states that if the participant had “come to us in good
        faith…I would have said okay.”
    f) Email 16 June 2022 sent by you to participant, Ms Abraham, “I would like all of your
        outstanding invoices paid immediately please or I will report this to the NDIS”.
14. The Commission holds additional information that indicates that you played a central role in the
    alleged fraudulent activities engaged in by Forward Focused Group, in particular the submission
    of false claims for payments to the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme). I am of the
    view that this demonstrates a failure by you to act with integrity, honesty and transparency.

15. It is alleged that Forward Focused Group were knowingly submitting false claims for payments to
    the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly causing a
    loss to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The Commission is aware of
    questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.

16. The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on dates
    known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider generated
    invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the necessary
    Plans or Services Agreements in place.




                                 NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 5 of 7
                                              1131
                                                                                          ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                          T57

17. The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
    show that you were withholding information from other staff of the Provider on potentially
    fraudulent invoices. By way of example,
    a) Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from former employee to the Chief Financial Officer raising
        questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for participant that
        Forward Focused Group had not provided. In your response dated 9 May 2022, you replied
        “Whilst I appreciate your thoroughness in dealing with clients please remember that I do
        work on participants as well. I keep my work and my notes private as sometimes they may
        be about staff or complaints or other work clients have requested that they have advised
        not to be disclosed to other due to privacy and dignity … I am aware that you have recently
        gained access to some portals and kindly ask that you do not access these as they go against
        our policy and procedures.”

18. The Commission holds records of correspondence between you and participants which reflects a
    lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-payment or broken trust as
    grounds for you to immediately terminate engagements with participants. By way of example,

    a) Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
       participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due to
       “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The Commission holds
       a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated 27 March 2022, requesting
       that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the Provider cease.

19. The information available to the Commission indicates that you engage in dishonest and
    intimidating conduct that falls below the standard expected from key personnel of a registered
    NDIS provider and the director of a company that provides disability supports and services.

Submissions

20. The Commission received a submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission date).

21. Your submission notes that a request for further time and further information were not
    responded to however, the Commission did respond to this request. This is evidenced by the
    Commission’s acceptance of the submission on 30 November 2022 (the extended submission
    date).

22. Your submission responds to a number of facts and information we raised in the notice of
    intention to the provider dated 19 October 2022.

23. Your submission confirms that you held relevant information in relation to the issues identified.

24. Your submission denies all allegations.

25. I have carefully considered the information provided in your submissions alongside previous
    information outlined in the letter dated 19 October 2022. I note your submission does not
    provide the Commission with additional evidence to support your position.

26. Having weighed all the evidence at hand I have formed the reasonable belief that you have
    contravened, are contravening, or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, as per section
    73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                    Page 6 of 7
                                               1132
                                                                                           ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                           T57

27. As part of my considerations I am obliged to consider the NDIS Act. Section 3 of the NDIS Act
    contains the objects of the NDIS Act, which includes:

            a. Supporting the independence and social and economic participation of people with
               disability.

            b. Enabling people with disability to exercise choice and control in the pursuit of their
               goals and the panning and delivery of their supports.

            c. Protecting and preventing people with disability from experiencing harm arising
               from poor quality or unsafe supports or services provided under the National
               Disability Insurance Scheme.

28. In giving effect to the objects of the NDIS Act, including those outlined above, section 3(3)(b)
    requires regard to be had to the need to ensure financial sustainability of the National Disability
    Insurance Scheme (NDIS). Similarly, as set out in NDIS Act section 4(17) it is the intention of the
    Parliament that the Commissioner, amongst others, have regard to the need to ensure financial
    sustainability of the NDIS in performing functions and exercising powers under the NDIS Act.

29. The need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be particularly vulnerable,
    and ensure they are kept safe, outweighs your interest in continuing to be involved in the
    provision of supports or services to people with a disability.

30. In determining whether to make a Banning Order for you, I weighed the following competing
    interests and concerns:
        a. The nature of your alleged breaches of the NDIS Act are serious, as they involve
            instances of verbal and emotional abuse involving threats towards vulnerable persons
            with disability to whom you were providing services and supports on a regular basis. The
            Commission holds information that suggested that you exerted undue influence over a
            number of participants that limited them from exercising their choice and control over
            their own supports and services.
        b. The Commission holds information that suggests that you have been using your position
            to exploit participants who are in vulnerable situations despite your knowledge of your
            obligations under the NDIS Act. For these reasons, I am concerned your breaches will
            continue to occur in future.

31. The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and quality
    of care expected from key personnel of NDIS providers when providing supports or services to
    people with disability. The information before me in relation to the protracted length of time
    over which the alleged conduct took place suggests that your conduct was systemic in nature.
    Allowing you to continue providing supports or services to people with disability poses a
    continuing risk to people with disability.

32. I am of the view that making a Banning Order against you would provide a strong level of
    protection for people with disability and maintain community confidence in the Scheme.

33. For the avoidance of doubt, the Banning Order will prevent you from having any involvement
    whether directly or indirectly in the delivery, coordination and management of NDIS-funded
    supports and services to people with disability.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                     Page 7 of 7
                                               1133
                                                                                            ATTACHMENT B

                                                                                                            T57

34. The ban will therefore preclude you from having any involvement in any other entity that
    provides supports and services to NDIS participants.

35. I accept that the making of a banning order for you will impact your ability to earn an income in
    the disability services sector, and I have taken this into account in my consideration to issue the
    banning order.

36. I consider your alleged conduct is entirely relevant to my assessment of your suitability to be
    involved in the provision of supports or services to NDIS participants, in considering the objects
    and principles of the NDIS Act. This is particularly the case as the parties impacted by your
    actions are NDIS participants.

37. I consider that a timeframe of five (5) years is appropriate and will provide a strong level of
    protection for people with disability.




Philip Halton
Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
Delegate of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commissioner

20 December 2022

Dear Mr Monks

                 Acknowledgment of request for internal review by Ms Vida Reid
                Section 100(2) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013

I refer to the email received by the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission on 25 January
2023 requesting an internal review of the decision dated 20 December 2022 to impose a
banning order against your client, Ms Vida Reid

Internal review process
A delegate of the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commissioner who was not involved in the making of
the original decision will conduct the internal review. The delegate will consider the merits of the
original decision and must then decide to do one of the following:
    • confirm the decision; or
    • vary the decision; or
    • set the decision aside and substitute a new decision.

Further submissions
In the email dated 25 January 2023, you did not explain why your client is seeking an internal
review or provide us with information in support of the request for review.

If your client wishes to make any submissions in support of her request for an internal review, or to
provide us with any information to consider in our internal review, please provide it by email to
Review@ndiscommission.gov.au by 5pm on 20 February 2023. If no submissions are received by this
date, we will proceed to make a decision on the internal review.

If your client chooses to make submissions, she should be aware that giving false and misleading
information to the NDIS Commission is a serious offence.

Due date for internal review decision
We are required to make a decision on the internal review within 90 days from the day we received
the request for review. This means our decision is due to be made by 24 April 2023.




                                              1136
                                                                                                  T59

Effect of internal review
In accordance with section 100 of the NDIS Act, a request for internal review does not affect the
operation of the original decision or prevent the taking of action to give effect to the original decision.
This means that the decision to impose a banning order will continue to apply until we make our
internal review decision.

Withdrawal
You can choose to withdraw this request for internal review at any time before we make our
decision on the review. You can withdraw the request by email to
Review@ndiscommission.gov.au or by telephone using the details below.

Personal information
In making this request for internal review and any submissions in support of the request, you may
provide us with personal information. Please consider the enclosed privacy notice in relation to any
personal information you provide.

Contact details and further information
Should you have any queries about the internal review process or anything I have said in this letter,
please do not hesitate to contact me by telephone on 02 9942 5726 or by email to
pascal.moussa@ndiscommission.gov.au.

Yours sincerely



Pascal Moussa
Lawyer
NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission

6 February 2023

PRIVACY NOTICE

The NDIS Commission is bound by the Australian Privacy Principles outlined in the Privacy Act 1988
(Cth) (Privacy Act), which regulate how entities may collect, use, disclose and store personal
information.
The NDIS Commission is also bound by the information management provisions of the NDIS Act.
These provide that if the NDIS Commission holds information about a person in its records then that
information is regarded as ‘protected Commission information’ under the National Disability
Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (NDIS Act). Unauthorised use and disclosure of protected Commission
information by any person is a criminal offence under the NDIS Act.
Collection of personal information
The NDIS Commission may collect personal information from you to process and decide your request
for internal review.
If you do not provide us with the personal information sought by us in relation to your review, the
NDIS Commission may not be able to process your request for an internal review.
Use and disclosure of personal/protected information
Personal information obtained from you in relation to your review will be used and disclosed for the
purposes of processing and deciding your request for internal review.
We will only use or disclose your personal information for another purpose or in any other
circumstances if:
    •   you consent, or
    •   it is required or authorised by law, including under the Privacy Act or NDIS Act.

The NDIS Act authorises the use and disclosure of protected Commission information (including
personal information) in certain circumstances, which may include:
    •  disclosing information to the National Disability Insurance Agency (NDIA), including for the
       purposes of notifying the NDIA of matters relating to the registration of an NDIS provider
    • using or disclosing information for the purposes of performing the NDIS Commissioner’s
       functions, such as carrying out its compliance and enforcement functions
    • disclosing information to other relevant parties, including other Commonwealth, state or
       territory agencies, regulatory bodies or professional associations
    • disclosing personal information to an overseas recipient to support certain NDIS Commission
       functions.
Personal information obtained through the internal review process will be stored and held in
accordance with the NDIS Commission’s obligations under the Privacy Act and the Archives Act 1983
(Cth).
The NDIS Commission’s privacy policy
The NDIS Commission’s privacy policy (available at www.ndiscommission.gov.au/privacy) sets out
how the NDIS Commission complies with the Privacy Act. The policy describes:
    •   the kinds of personal information we collect and hold
    •   the purposes for which we collect, hold, use and disclose personal information




                                              1138
                                                                            T59

•   how you can seek access to and correct personal information
•   how we deal with any unauthorised disclosures of personal information
•   how to make a privacy complaint.

Dear Mr Monks


   Intention to take into account new information received by the NDIS Quality and Safeguards
                                           Commission
                            Internal review of banning order – Vida Reid


I refer to the email received by the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission (the NDIS Commission)
on 25 January 2023 requesting an internal review of the decision made on 20 December 2022 to
impose a banning order against your client, Ms Vida Reid.
I am the delegate that will be making the decision on the internal review.
I am due to make the internal review decision by 24 April 2023.
Intention to take into account new information and vary banning order
In considering the request for internal review, I have become aware of information not considered
by the original delegate relating to Ms Reid’s alleged involvement in a business with links to the
NDIS, Dream Big Employment Pty Ltd. I have set out details of the new information in Attachment A
to this letter.
The banning order currently prohibits Ms Reid from providing supports and services, directly or
indirectly, to NDIS funded participants for a period of five years (effective from 20 December 2022),
with the exception of Ms Reid being able to seek supports or services for herself and provide
supports or services to her son, Mr Alex Reid.
Taking into account the information at Attachment A, I intend on varying the banning order against
Ms Reid to make it explicit that she is to have no involvement with or in the NDIS sector, in any
capacity. This includes being prohibited from providing labour hire workers to support NDIS
providers or providing support, funding, guidance or assistance of any form to an NDIS provider.
I am writing now to give Ms Reid the opportunity to make any submissions to me in relation to both
this information and my proposed variation to the terms of the banning order.
If your client wishes to make submissions, please provide them by email to
review@ndiscommission.gov.au by 5pm on 5 April 2023. If no submissions are received by this
date, I will proceed to make the internal review decision.



                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                1

If your client wishes to request additional time to provide submissions, please submit such a request
via email to review@ndiscommission.gov.au by 31 March 2023.
If your client chooses to make submissions, she should be aware that giving false and misleading
information to the NDIS Commission is a serious offence.
Contact details
Should you have any queries about this matter, you can contact Ms Pascal Moussa by telephone to
02 9942 5726 or by email to pascal.moussa@ndiscommission.gov.au


Yours sincerely




Sian Leathem
Complaints Commissioner Executive
NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission


22 March 2023
